# The Caddie — Cold-Read Log

The Briggsy-proxy cold reader's verdict cards, newest first (mechanism:
[`.claude/skills/caddie/SKILL.md`](../../.claude/skills/caddie/SKILL.md)). **Every card here is
a SUBORDINATE, provisional stand-in** — any real Briggsy read of the same surface supersedes
its card and gets folded into the taste corpus + scored on [the tape](tape.md). A card is never
logged as, or mistaken for, his N=1 verdict.

**The batched-oracle law (Briggsy's 2026-07-11 grant — *"I don't review your code — why am I
reviewing your content?"*):** a chaired card SHIPS; there is no per-surface park for his eye.
His eye audits at product cadence — any-time reads plus the pre-exposure **Briggsy-bar
gauntlet** (nothing leaves the repo for anyone else until it clears) — and his findings staple
back onto the cards that missed them. *(Cards dated before 2026-07-11 were filed under the
earlier parking law; their dispositions are historical records.)*

Dispositions: **PILOT-CLEARED** (the default for a readable surface — ships; reason + any fix
logged; the tape row stays open for his audit) · **HARD-FLAG — HELD FOR THE ORACLE** (since
2026-09-30, `9ff5496b`: a surviving finding that questions whether a rendered figure, a date or
duration, a sufficiency claim, or the state of the household's plan actually holds — the card
cannot be PILOT-CLEARED while it is open, and becomes PILOT-CLEARED once the oracle closes it —
the figure measured through the engine, never re-argued from the copy; [the skill](../../.claude/skills/caddie/SKILL.md)'s chair step 2,
⚑ THE HOLDS RULE) · **PARKED-FOR-BRIGGSY** (the exception:
one-way doors, framing-level forks, or genuine pilot doubt — reason logged) ·
**BLOCKED-UNREACHABLE** (the surface cannot be rendered live; never read from source).

---

## 2026-11-13 (b9-6) — build 2's U11 Caddie record, walk 7: walk 6's round and the pair fix on the committed tree (`dc9ff19c`: `e6e9c364`, `3000a04c`, `865e7426`, `360bea64`, `5f96411d`) — `seed:`/`vault:datesavedrisen` · `seed:`/`vault:datesavedrisenshort` · `seed:`/`vault:datesavedrisenlater` · `seed:`/`vault:datesavedstale` · `seed:`/`vault:datesavedtypedmoved` (new: C retyped; landing, every door, unlock → gate → verdict), `vault:datesavedtyped` and `vault:datesavedfourbuckets` (unlock → gate → verdict), `intake:typedbelow` and `intake:kept`, REAL+PHONE — walk 7 `temp/caddie/caddie-u11-b9-5-walk7` (captured by b9-5, 28/28 in 5.5 m, pinned to the P-cores; 11 fresh Opus xhigh seats by b9-5 — a cold first look on each of the A, B, C, S, T and TM gates, a phone reader on C's whole gate, an unprimed spouse on the six couples, a copy-law finder, a hunter, a CVD screener; then b9-6: one engine measurement for R1's holds rule and 8 refuters (b9-5's eight had died on an API 403 before reading a file); every seat a de-authored Agent writing its report to a file; no workflow journal)

**surface:** walk 6's round: R1's typed-figure line on the Result and the gate (`3000a04c`), the paragraph's labels drawn as parts and the gate's route under its two buttons (`e6e9c364`), B's sheet without "until" (`865e7426`), the round review's fixes (`360bea64`) and the pair fix (`5f96411d`: where a moved left over and R1's line speak together, each figure names the figure it rests on). The phone copy matches the laptop's in every state.

**firstImpression:** (A gate) *"calm and honest, but heavy… wondering if I did something wrong"*; (B) *"calm and honest but lawyerly and dense"*; (C) *"polite and careful but hedges a lot… uneasy and tired"*; (S) *"the long left-hand text wore me out"*; (T) *"I trust it more because it says outright that my answer 'can look better than it really is'"*; (TM) *"calm and careful… a little uneasy and fuzzy on the details"*; (C, phone, whole gate) *"I wanted a quick 'yes, still right' and got a lecture I only half followed"*.

**Walk 6's open questions (the panel's answers):**
1. **The labels as parts — NOW MET.** All five readers asked (A, B, C, S, P) read "This answer now" / "If a working year costs $X" / the route's heading as headings (walk 6: 0 of 5). The labels are the council's ruled parts (`wf_22858e04-f7a`), so the residue is a note for their next ruling, not a fix here.

T: "a roundabout way to say it"; TM: "needing a paragraph to explain two buttons is a smell".
3. **The pair fix reads.** The spouse on TM states both first-year figures with their bases ($26,985 at $84,000; $5,985 at $105,000); TM's cold reader sorts all three left-over figures. Residue: R3.
4. **Still "a wall"** on every gate. The phone reader: the balances the buttons confirm come after a wall about something the buttons don't fix (a re-find of (q)).

**toneVerdict:** HARD-FLAG. One high in both lanes, a ⚑ holds finding measured through the engine (R1); four medium in both (R2 on TM, R4's gap, R5, R8 (a)); four medium in tone (R3 (a), R3 (c), R6 (b), R6 (c)); six low (R2 on A's retype, R4's "adds up to", R6 (a), R7 (a), R8 (b) in the harness, R8 (c)). **findings (refuter-verified; the chair checked every surviving string in the bundle and R1 against `u11-walk7\measure\out-r1.json`; rules from `taste-corpus.md`):**
- **R1 High, both — TM's line names the short years but never says the answer saves in them.** TM's gate and Result: "At that cost, each working year from 2029 through 2032 comes up $3,652 to $4,015 short." Nothing says what the answer does in those years. "Adds at most $16,985" (Result) and "adds up to $16,985" (gate) both allow nothing, and the sheet's method line ("if a year comes up short, the difference comes out of savings") gives the couple a way to read the shortfalls as already taken. The out-of-date arms say it (B: "leaves those shortfalls out of this answer", `src/ui/copy.ts:3283`; C: "still counted in your savings for every year after", `:3321`); the typed arms (`workingCostTypedBelowAtQuoteShort`, `…LaterShort`, `:3421–3430`) do not, so the household credited more is told less (rules 2, 8; E17). **Measured** (`u11-walk7\measure\R1-MEASURE.md`): TM credits $16,985 in every working year, 2029–2032 included, where the honest "Yes" at $105,000 credits nothing there and draws the shortfall; both headlines read "about 8 of 10", but the honest one only because 0.75 rounds up, where TM sits at 0.80 (point +5.3, higher at every offset). B retyped (a code path with no capture) reads "about 8 of 10" against the honest "about 7 of 10", walk 6's B arm again. Fix, in both typed short composers right after the short-years sentence: "Your answer takes none of those shortfalls from your savings: at your $84,000 figure, those years leave money over, and it adds $16,985 to your savings in each of them." The credit comes from the run's own rule (each year's left over at the typed figure, capped), never assumed. High, not blocker: no false date shows (TM: "No fuck-off date…"), and the line already states the direction.
- **R2 Medium (narrowed), both, on TM; low on A's retype — the sheet's W note and left-over seat give only the typed-figure numbers.** TM's sheet names $26,985, "choose 'All of it' once more… at most $26,985" and "leave this as it is", never $5,985 or the short years; A's retype sheet never names $10,985 (rules 1, 37, 38). The modal hides the Result's pair, and the Result's door opens this exact row. Fix: where the sheet's W note shows under a credited "saved" answer, end it with R1's own at-spending sentences in the Result's words (TM: "At $105,000 a year, your first working year, 2026, has $5,985 left over. At that cost, each working year from 2029 through 2032 comes up $3,652 to $4,015 short."; A: "At $90,000 a year, your first working year, 2026, has $10,985 left over."), plus R1's new sentence where it applies; the seat then has a twin, so its line names its basis ("After everything above, at your $84,000 figure, your first working year has $26,985 left over.") and so does the moved clause ("At your $84,000 figure, it's $26,985 now"). Killed: "the pair fix missed the seat" (the seat had no twin to label) and the leave-as-is "no statement check" (its condition carries dollars; the missing $36,985 total is (aa): add this sighting).
- **R4 Medium (narrowed), both — T's $6,000 is left to subtract.** T's gate, T's Result and A's retype Result: "It adds up to $16,985 a year to your savings. At $90,000 a year, your first working year, 2026, has $10,985 left over." The hedge never says how much (E17); A's out-of-date page does. Fix, T's three surfaces: "This answer adds at most $16,985 a year to your savings. At $90,000 a year, your first working year, 2026, has $10,985 left over. That year, this answer adds $6,000 your pay doesn't really leave over." (Tied to 2026: exact there, and later years at $90,000 leave more.) The other arm, "adds up to" read as "totals", is low tone: the cap binds in every T year, so "totals" is true; it says "at most" in the fix, and on TM's gate it rides R3 (c).
- **R5 Medium (narrowed), both — B's sheet never says its working years come up short.** B's seat (sheet line 182) and its method twin (line 88): "Updating it to your spending figures or more won't change that: at that cost your first working year has nothing left over." "Nothing left over" reads as breaking even, where every year 2026–2032 draws $3,652 to $4,015; and the only thing said about updating, under the buttons, is what it won't do (rule 8). One string covers year 0 short and exactly $0 (`src/ui/copy.ts:566-575`); the Result already splits them. Fix: split the seat's string by year 0's sign, today's words for exactly $0; short: "Your figure for a working year needs an update, above. No money left over is added to your savings. At $105,000 a year, each working year from 2026 through 2032 comes up $3,652 to $4,015 short, so nothing is left over to add. Updating to $105,000 or more won't add any, but it puts those shortfalls into your answer." Method twin, no dollars: "…won't change that: at that cost your first working year comes up short. Updating puts those shortfalls into your answer." Killed: "no $105,000" (the question two lines above supplies it).
- **R8 (a) Medium (narrowed), both — "figure" means the left over in the saved and moved method lines.** "…up to your first working year's figure" (saved, `src/ui/copy.ts:1791`) and "…at most the smaller of the two figures" (moved, `:1793`), with "your figure for a working year… that figure" (the $84,000) in the next sentence; no dollars until the seat (rule 1). The misread is the rosy one: the cap read as the $84,000. A and C's waiting lines are clean. Fix, in words only (the method lines carry no dollars by design, `copy.ts`'s comment above `assumptionWorkingYearCheckLeftOverNotAsked`): "…up to your first working year's left over." / "…at most the smaller of the two left overs."
- **R3 (a) Medium (narrowed), tone — four figures in one sentence on TM's Result.** "At your $84,000 figure, your first working year has $26,985 left over now, not the $16,985 you answered at, so each working year adds at most $16,985…" The spouse needed three reads (rule 5, E20), and the Result never says what the couple answered, where the gate does (rule 37). Fix, the gate's form: "You said all of it goes into your savings when your first working year had $16,985 left over. At your $84,000 figure, it has $26,985 left over now. So each working year adds at most $16,985 of its left over to your savings, the smaller of the two." Killed: "$16,985 reads as a working-year figure" (no reader did).
- **R3 (c) Medium (narrowed), tone — TM's gate says the cap twice.** R1's "It adds up to $16,985 a year to your savings" comes before the moved line explains it, beside "$5,985 left over" for the same year (rule 6). TM's reader: "a math error until paragraph two explained that $16,985 is a cap". Fix: R1's bare cap sentence leaves TM's gate, as it already has on TM's Result; the cap stays once, in the moved line beside its cause; two lines still (one cause, one line). If R1's fix puts $16,985 back into R1's line, it names the years, not the cap. Killed: "has changed since you answered" landing after "$5,985" (a paragraph break and the 2029–2032 sentence sit between).
- **R6 (b) Medium, tone — C's (and F's) "count some of it again" has no clear "it".** One shared sentence (`src/ui/copy.ts:612`) closes B's arms (where "brings none of the $16,985 back" gives "it" a referent) and C's (`src/intake/workingYearLeftOver.ts:447`), where it has none. The phone reader: "some of what?" Fix, C's arm only: "A lower figure could count more of the $16,985 again, but only by pricing a working year below what you spend."
- **R6 (c) Medium, tone — A's opening says $6,000 twice in one comparison.** "Your $84,000 figure is $6,000 below your spending, so it makes $6,000 a year more of your pay look left over than a working year at $90,000 would leave." A's reader: "I read this three times" (rules 2, 5). Fix: "Your $84,000 figure is $6,000 below your spending. So every working year looks to leave $6,000 more of your pay than it would at $90,000. In 2026 that's $16,985 at your $84,000 figure, against $10,985 at $90,000 a year."
- **R6 (a) Low (narrowed), tone — A's lower-figure sentence prices the gain, not the cost.** "A figure below $90,000 would add more, up to $16,985 a year, but the extra is money your pay doesn't really leave over." The spouse: "Then why offer it?" Fix, cost first, both figures kept: "A figure below $90,000 would count money your pay doesn't really leave over: up to $6,000 a year more than the $10,985, for as much as $16,985 a year."
- **R7 (a) Low (narrowed), tone — S's Keep condition counts spending in its own list.** "…only if the costs listed below come to $6,000 a year or less, on top of your $78,000 of spending." Meetable through "on top of", but the list opens with "what you spend". Fix: "…only if the costs listed below, other than what you spend, come to $6,000 a year or less on top of your $78,000 of spending."
- **R8 (b) Low, the harness — the CVD images stop at the first screen.** Every `cvd-*.png` is one viewport (1536×791 / 390×844); the phone gates' buttons and route, and the last label, cost list and door on A, B, C, F and S's answers, were never filtered (laptop gates covered). Fix: filter `fullpage.png` or the existing `crop-paycheck-note` / `crop-reentry-door` crops in `e2e/caddie-walk.spec.ts`, as it already does for the band and ladder.
- **R8 (c) Low, accessibility — only the affirm button is described.** `ReEntry.tsx:110-120` points the affirm button's `aria-describedby` at the scope sentence; the other button has none, and `reentry.test.tsx:1096` / `:1107` pin that on purpose. Read why before changing it; if nothing forbids it, point both at the span ("Neither button" holds for both). The harness should record accessible descriptions (Playwright's aria snapshot omits them).
- **Killed whole:** R3 (b) ("the smaller of the two" sounds cautious: the next sentence gives the $5,985 with its basis); R7 (b) ("this answer" points back to the paragraph's "Your answer"; the label residue is question 1's).
- **Re-found, filed already:** copylaw H3 (the kept PA page's "your new state answer changes which state's income tax…" under "is already counted") = the Tier 0 *On the date route the North Carolina / Pennsylvania clause says "your state income tax" with no scope…* ((ak): add this sighting); copylaw M2 + cvd F3 ("Either choice below leads to where you can change it": at 1536 px the buttons sit beside, not below; "it" is the figure or the answer) = (r) and the round's item 8; the spouse on C ($21,000 more cost, an $11,000 drop) = (al)'s rise arm (the filing covers the arithmetic, not only the naming); the spouse on T and TM ("nothing on the answer page tells me to add premiums"; "why isn't a working year just our spending?") = (l), (n); T's "A's numbers look identical, yet A says no date and T says 2035" = the Tier 0 retype entry's residual; R2's leave-as-is total = (aa).

## 2026-11-12 (b9-1) — build 2's U11 Caddie record, walk 6: walk 5's fix round on the committed tree (`aa29f955`, `ec3514a7`'s words) — `seed:`/`vault:datesavedrisen` · `seed:`/`vault:datesavedrisenshort` · `seed:`/`vault:datesavedrisenlater` · `seed:`/`vault:datesavedstale` (landing, every door, unlock → gate → verdict), `intake:kept` (ten states) and `intake:typedbelow` (new: the retype, `aa29f955`), REAL+PHONE — walk 6 `temp/caddie/caddie-u11-b9-2-walk6` (captured by b9-2 on 2026-11-09, 19/20: `intake:kept` at REAL failed at `result-kept-stale-both` twice; b9-1 added its six REAL states on 2026-11-12 from a passing run on a fresh server, and the failure did not reproduce in three runs, none showing a render loop (`u11-walk6\panel\CHAIR-NOTES.md`, its foot); 7 fresh Opus xhigh seats by b9-2 — a cold first look on each of the four gates, an unprimed spouse, a copy-law finder, a hunter; then b9-1: one engine measurement for R1's holds rule and 11 refuters, workflow `wf_033ffe40-409`, every seat writing its report to a file)

**surface:** walk 5's round on the working-year paragraph: each run-in label on its own line in semibold ((bi)'s form cure), the reason beside "adds none of it" ((bh)), the door's sentences after the labels ((bj), (bk)), the figure-named legend, and the Tier 0 typed-below note (`workingYearCostTypedBelowNote`) on the W step and the details sheet's W row. The phone copy matches the laptop's in every state.

**firstImpression:** (`vault:datesavedrisen` gate) *"…The left side is a wall of text about $84,000 vs $90,000 that I didn't expect on a 'quick look' page. The tone is calm and almost lawyerly. I trust the right side. The left leaves me tired, a little worried I've done something wrong, and unsure which button is safe."* (`vault:datesavedrisenlater` gate) *"…The tone is honest, almost lawyerly. I trust it, but I'm anxious and worn out: I can't tell whether my answer is too rosy or too gloomy, or what to do."*

**The five questions walk 6 owed (the panel's answers; R6 measured the fifth):**
1. **The labels as parts — NOT MET; clinical.** All four cold looks and the spouse read "This answer now:" / "If a working year costs $X:" as phrases or sentence starts, not headings, even on their own lines in semibold. 5/5 "clinical".
2. **Does the reason end "a bug?" — yes, but it now reads as a penalty** (A, C, S: "penalty"; B: "sensible but worded like a penalty") → F6.
3. **Does anyone still reach for the retype — YES.** A: "tempted to put $84,000 back just to clear the warning"; C the same; the spouse on B: "tempting because it makes the answer look better" → R1, R4.
4. **Does the gate's door sentence send anyone to the green button as "Yes" — YES.** A and S would press "Still about right" thinking it confirms the figure; every seat lost the thread at "Neither button on this page…" (re-found: (q), (r)).
5. **Lighter or only longer — ONLY LONGER, and mostly by words.** `.reentry-notes` at REAL, walk 5 → walk 6: A 323 → 546 px, B 372 → 620, C 447 → 670, S 248 → 471. R6 re-rendered both bundles' paragraphs in the project's own font at the captured column (every height within 1 px): about 89% of the added lines are added words (+8 lines a host; the breaks add 1–2, the semibold 0). The door holds 61–64% of the added words on A, B and C (C's 53 → 120 words) and 85% on S. C's note ends at 841 px on a 791 px frame: the two lines out of frame are the door's last sentence, the one that names "Yes, that's about right".

**toneVerdict:** HARD-FLAG. One blocker in both lanes, a ⚑ holds finding measured through the engine (R1); one high in both lanes (R2); three medium in both (R3, R4, F11); two medium in tone (R6, F6); two low in tone (R5, F7). Two killed (F4 a re-find, F5).

**findings (refuter-verified; the chair checked R1's B arm in `measure\refuter-R1-B\out-r1.json`, the measurement against both bundle headlines, and F5's seed — `datecoststale` = `withConfirmedW(stillWorking)` at $78,000, no left-over answer, `src/ui/devSeeds.ts:1318-1321`):**
- **R1 Blocker (narrowed), both — the retype's answer page is rosier than the honest update and says nothing.** The walk's claim that the retype FLIPS A's headline died: the honest "Yes" at $90,000 crowns the same "about 9 years out — around 2035", "about 9 of 10 odds" (`measure\R1-MEASURE.md`; typing $90,000 is the same run). What survives: on A the retype's lower bound is 0.87 against the honest 0.85, which sits exactly on the bar with zero margin, and it credits $16,985 a working year against $10,985; on B (`datesavedrisenshort`) the retype reads "The nearest any year came was about 8 of 10" against the honest "about 7 of 10". No line on the answer page or the gate names the typed $84,000, though the KEPT household with the same gap keeps one (copylaw F1). The typed-below note sits under the question only, below the first frame in the sheet (1059–1976 px) and the step (331–1273 px), where "about 9 of 10 odds" sits in frame above it. Appended to the Tier 0 *A figure typed below today's spending clears W's stale line for good…* with the measurement and the refuter's prescription: while a typed figure sits below today's spending, the Result and the gate keep one line in the kept line's slot and form, door "Update your figure for a working year", naming both figures and the dollars, above "This range prices your full budget…". No stamp, no withheld credit (D7); the stamp stays that entry's council call.
- **R2 High, both — A's lead names a harm A does not have.** "Your $84,000 figure makes any working year your pay doesn't cover look cheaper than it really is": no A year falls short at $90,000; what $84,000 does on A is put $6,000 a year more of pay on the left-over line. Two readers took it as "we have short years". Fix, only where no working year is short at today's spending and a left over was saved: "Your $84,000 figure is $6,000 below your spending, so it makes $6,000 a year more of your pay look left over than a working year at $90,000 would leave." (Never the hunter's present-tense "more looks left over to save", which "adds none of it" contradicts.) For the council: the measurement meets constraint 3's "until measured" for A at one point only — the honest update moves A from no date to 2035 at zero margin; above $90,000 (Alex's premium for Sam, entered nowhere) and S are unmeasured.
- **R3 Medium (narrowed), both — B's "until you update your figure" points at a figure below $105,000.** No reader still believed "it comes back" after "$105,000 or more adds none", but B's readers lost the thread at the pair. Fix, B only: "This answer now:" stops at "…this answer adds none of it to your savings."; the "If a working year costs $105,000:" close gets A's and C's reason ("Since a working year almost always costs at least what you spend, updating your figure to $105,000 or more brings none of the $16,985 back, because your first working year, 2026, comes up short at that cost."); copylaw F9's third "look cheaper" becomes the amount ("…comes up $3,652 to $4,015 short, and your $84,000 figure leaves those shortfalls out of this answer."). The same B-only ending on the sheet's method row and left-over seat ("Until you update…, no money left over is added").
- **R4 Medium (narrowed), both — the paragraph prices only the honest figure.** The kept step's half died (it names $84,000 twice, and the retype draws the field note). Fix: one sentence at the end of each "If a working year costs $X:" part — A: "A figure below $90,000 would add more, up to $16,985 a year, but the extra is money your pay doesn't really leave over."; B and C: "A lower figure could count some of it again, but only by pricing a working year below what you spend."
- **F11 Medium (narrowed), both — S's three choices in one sentence.** The missing "If a working year costs $78,000:" died (ruled absent where spending fell). **findings (verified by the chair against the bundle; the stale composer `src/intake/workingYearLeftOver.ts:407`; the seed `stillWorking`, `src/ui/devSeeds.ts:144`: Alex 58 working, Sam 60 retired at 58, covered by Alex's plan at work; door 1's "Your answer uses about $X a year": A $90,000, B and C $105,000, S $78,000; every rendered figure holds):**

(S: "until you confirm or change your figure").

There, choose…".
- **(bk) Medium, both: the definition offers "Yes" before the reasons for "No", in words that misread, and S's carries no condition.** On A, B and C "Yes" reads as the default; B read "health premiums" as a plan bought on one's own; the spouse stalled on "whether taken from pay"; S's "Keep $84,000 a year" sits above the sheet's list (`crop-w-row-1.png`), so a couple can keep a spending-only figure without passing it. S's half died (its note scopes the $84,000). Fix, the same on all four, first under "This answer now:": "Once you've both stopped working, it already uses your spending of $X a year." ("about" dropped: the note printed the same figure exactly).

## 2026-11-09 (b9-5) — build 2's U11 Caddie record, walk 4: the fix round (as)–(aw)(b) on the committed tree (`98b5ad17`) — `seed:`/`vault:datesavedrisen` · `seed:`/`vault:datesavedrisenshort` · `seed:`/`vault:datesavedrisenlater` · `seed:`/`vault:datesavedstale` (landing, every door, unlock → gate → verdict) and `intake:kept` (ten states; its two Result states re-read), REAL+PHONE — walk 4 `temp/caddie/caddie-u11-b9-5-walk4` (18/18 in 4.1 m; 7 fresh Opus xhigh seats — a cold first look on each of the four gates, an unprimed spouse on the four couples, a copy-law finder, a hunter — then 9 refuters over the 9 new findings; every seat a de-authored Agent writing its report to a file, so nothing truncated; no workflow journal)

**surface:** the working-year paragraph after (as) (the shortfall drawn from savings with its tax), (at) (the gain's reach), (ap)'s cure (one fact per dollar sentence, the stored figure dated), (au) ("confirm or change"), (av) and (aw)(b), on every landing, gate and verdict; `datesavedstale`'s fallen arm (its clause changed too) walked for the first time since the go-live; `intake:kept`'s two Result states. The phone copy matches the laptop's on every state (the chart's axis labels aside).

**firstImpression:** (`vault:datesavedrisen` gate) *"…The left paragraph is a wall of text about $84,000 versus $90,000. It made me feel something is wrong with my plan, and I couldn't quite tell what. I trust the right side. The left side leaves me unsure and a little uneasy."* (`vault:datesavedrisenshort` gate) *"…my answer probably looks better than it really is. The tone is calm and honest, a little lawyerly, and the middle paragraph is long. I trust that it flagged the gap. I feel a bit uneasy and slightly lost in the math."* (`vault:datesavedrisenlater` gate) *"…The tone is calm and polite, but there are too many numbers in one block. I come away uneasy and a little overwhelmed. I'm fairly sure I should NOT press the big green 'Still about right' button."* (`vault:datesavedstale` gate) *"…one dense paragraph about $84,000, $78,000 and $16,985 that I had to read three times. I mostly trust the page, but I came away unsure what I'm confirming and what happens when I click."* Every readback matched the DOM, figure for figure and in order.

**The five questions walk 4 owed (TODO, item 1 Phase C):**
1. **The dated figure — MET on A and B, NOT MET on C.** A's cold reader and the spouse both made $16,985 − $6,000 = $10,985; B's both made $84,000 + $16,985 = $100,985 and $105,000 − $100,985 = $4,015. On C, four seats (the cold look, the spouse, the copy-law reader at high, the hunter) could not reconcile a $21,000 rise with a fall from $16,985 to $5,985; the spouse: "This is where I stopped trusting the numbers." Dating the figure does not explain a change the page never names: Sam's $10,000 a year of alimony, begun since the answer and ending after 2028, appears on no page and no sheet. So (al)'s rise arm is NOT closed by "When you answered" (walk 3's card expected it to be); raised to high below.
2. **"Taken from your savings, plus any income tax on taking it out" — MET.** No seat read "tread water". B's cold reader: "Before that sentence, I'd have guessed a short year just means I save nothing, or I trim spending… I feel informed and a little alarmed." The spouse: "alarming, but in a useful way." The residual is the word "any" ((bg), low).
3. **"In each working year that doesn't come up short" — MET on its own terms.** Both C readers counted three gain years (2027 and 2028 by inference) against four short ones and said plainly they could not net it ("close to a wash… Can't tell from this page"). Neither found the years from 2033 on that the phrase also covers ((be)).
4. **"Confirm or change" as the act — PARTLY.** No seat read "we looked, it's checked"; the spouse's "done" is the right one ("If that paragraph is still there, I'm not done"). But "confirm … your $84,000 figure" sent two readers looking for a door A, B and C do not have ((ba)), and all four cold readers split between the gate's two buttons ((q), (r)).
5. **Density — NOT MET.** Every seat: each sentence easy alone, the whole only longer. The threads broke at the connectives: "So checking your figure at $X or more adds…" (all three risen cold looks; the refuter counts 9 of 9 reads stumbling), "Until you confirm…, none of it is added to your savings" (read as the saving stopping), and C's "…so this answer can look better". Two readers asked for the bottom line first. Every surviving fix below adds a sentence, so the form question went to the council before any copy (see disposition).

**toneVerdict:** HARD-FLAG. One high finding in both lanes ((az)) and (al)'s rise arm raised to high. No rosier-than-true reading survives its refuter: the hunter's on `datesavedstale` died as unmeasured ((bf)).

**findings (verified by the chair against the bundle, the W step's guard in `src/ui/copy.ts` (`errWorkingCostStaleBelowQuote`) and the money picture at the quote: b9-2's `u11-ae\probe-ledger.out.txt` / `probe-latershort.out.txt`; every rendered figure holds):**
- **(az) High, both: the comparison sentence loses every reader, and one act has three names.** "So checking your figure at $90,000 or more adds at most $10,985 a year to your savings." (B: "…adds none of that $16,985"; C: "…adds at most $5,985 a year…, in each working year that doesn't come up short"). Its starting point ("none of it is added") sits two sentences up on A and C and nowhere on B, and the dated $16,985 offers a second one, so "adds" was read against the wrong figure: "Raising my spending ADDS to savings?" (C's cold look); "If I confirm $84,000, do I get $16,985 back instead?" (A's). The act is named "confirm or change your $84,000 figure", "checking your figure at $X" and "where you can change it", and the sheet says "checked". Refuter: survives, high, both. Fix: one name for the act, defined once in the sheet's own button words; the starting point inside the comparison sentence ("…instead of none today"); no "So".
- **(ba) Medium (narrowed), tone: "confirm … your $84,000 figure" names a door A, B and C do not have.** There the sheet's "Yes, that's about right" uses today's figure ($90,000 or $105,000), no Keep door is offered, and leaving $84,000 shows "Your $84,000 a year is below your spending now: choose 'Yes, that's about right' to use $90,000 a year, or type a new figure." So no button reaches a rosier answer (the copy-law reader's blocker condition is not met), and two readers went looking for the door. The council's red team then found that typing 84,000 by hand does reach one (see disposition): the refuter had rated that path as the household's own number. S's sheet does offer "Keep $84,000 a year", so S keeps its words. The button "Confirm or change your figure" opens the sheet rather than confirming anything (E12a's shape).
- **(bb) Medium, tone, the gloomy side: "none of it is added to your savings" reads as the household's saving stopping.** A's cold look: "I chose to save it and it's being thrown away? It reads like a penalty"; the spouse: "On first read I thought our savings had stopped." Refuter: survives. Fix: the answer as the actor ("this answer adds none of it…"), and the same for the sentences that say "adds … to your savings".
- **(bc) Medium (narrowed), tone: C's "Each year's shortfall is taken from your savings…, so this answer can look better than it really is" skips its middle.** The present tense sits inside a what-if, and the cause — this answer, still at $84,000, has no short year and takes nothing — is never said. C's cold look lost the thread exactly there. No seat read it rosy. Fix: "would be taken", then "Still at $84,000 a year, this answer takes none of them from your savings, so it can look better than it really is." B's "is taken" the same tense, its lean already tied to the figure.
- **(bd) Medium (narrowed), tone, the gloomy side: B never says what this answer does with the $16,985 now.** A, C and S say it; B leaves it out on purpose ("until you confirm" would promise a gain B cannot get), so "you chose to save all of it" then "adds none" reads as $21,000 a year of error against a true $3,652–$4,015 plus tax. Fix: state the present without a promise ("This answer adds none of it to your savings, and at $105,000 or more it still adds none.").
- **(be) Medium (narrowed), tone: C's gain years must be inferred.** The phrase is true. The hunter's fix ("in 2026 through 2028") would be false: from 2033, once Social Security starts, a working year has money left over again at $105,000. Fix: name both ("Those years are 2026 through 2028, and any year from 2033 on that either of you still works"), from the ledger, never typed.
- **(bf) Medium (narrowed), both: `datesavedstale` gives no direction, and both real readers built on a false premise.** The cold look (60%) and the hunter's couple read the answer as running on $84,000 of SPENDING; door 1 says it uses about $78,000, and pay covers a working year at either figure. The hunter's "2033 is the floor, a year sooner is ours" died: the payoff is unmeasured (E17's fill) and the floor needs the working-year costs door 5 lists. Fix candidates: the premise in one sentence, then A's at-quote pair at $78,000 with the cap at the $16,985 answered (never $22,985), then the either-way sentence. This is (al)'s fallen arm, sharpened; its direction rides (ax).
- **(bg) Low (narrowed), tone: "plus any income tax on taking it out" reads as maybe none.** $900,000 of this household's $1,100,000 is pre-tax. The dollar figure, "most of it" and the 10% penalty halves died (a path statistic the page doesn't have; the sheet discloses the penalty). Fix: "plus income tax on what comes out of pre-tax accounts" (still true under a Roth-first order).
- **Raised: (al)'s rise arm, medium → high (re-found by four seats, see question 1).** The figures hold (oracle: $26,985 − $21,000 = $5,985, with the alimony). The page must name the change, or today's left over at $84,000, in the money picture's words.
- **Re-found, filed already:** (ax) the either-way sentence names no direction (the spouse lost the thread there; A's cold look at 55% "wanted the page to say which way"); (q) and (r) the gate's buttons (all four cold looks; the N9 refuter: add "Balances still about right" to (r)'s candidates); the register's *On a stale W the W row hides its money picture* (the hunter: no sheet shows any figure the paragraph quotes); (aw)(a) "If you're still working" (one of them has stopped); (l) the $90,000 leaves out premiums; the Tier 0 PA scope entry ((ak); `intake:kept`'s "already counted" beside "should hold"); the em-dash and "You said yes" / "changed since" (older sentences, U12).
- **Killed:** "Keep this answer" as a launderer (saving confirms nothing; the paragraph returns with its door); "at most $10,985 a year" as false in later years (the tool's credit is capped at year 0's print, R8); the kept arm's "can look better" against A's "either way" (the kept household has no saved answer, so the spending-alone lean is true by mechanism: the build's recorded refinement); the hunter's floor reading on `datesavedstale` (above).

Found: the fallen couple's "2033 is the floor, a year sooner is ours" (died as unmeasured); C's figures (they hold; the comprehension half is (al)'s). Came up empty on: a look-and-close counting as done, a one-off reading of the shortfall, the phone hiding or reordering anything.

On C: "Spending went up $21,000, but what's left over only dropped $11,000. Where did the other $10,000 go?" On A: "One sentence says the answer is too rosy, the next says it's missing our savings, and then it says 'either way.' Which way is it for us?" On S: "No sentence lost me. The problem is what it doesn't say: whether $84,000 makes our 2033 date too early or too late." Asked to keep $84,000: "No, not on A, B or C."

(no threshold, no "worse"), and ruled it a framing change. **findings (verified by the chair against the bundle, the source and U11's record):**

So the clause does not defuse a real rosy lean here. Instead: (1) **the stated direction is wrong for this household** — the line says the answer "can look better than it really is", while it reads gloomier until a working year passes **$100,985** ($84,000 + the $16,985 printed on it), where year 0 turns short; five seats raised it, the refuter narrowed it to medium (P2: a false alarm, not a false calm — "None of the $16,985 … is added" does give the other direction in dollars); (2) **the clause's $16,985 promises more than a check returns** — "Until that figure is checked, none of the $16,985 a year you chose to save is added" reads as "once checked, $16,985", while a check at today's $90,000 credits at most $10,985 (the refuter: survives, medium; the moved line after the check corrects it, off this screen). Both are copy, prescribed in TODO.
- **Medium, the row's label after a unit tap on a kept figure** (copy-law and calm seats at blocker, hunter and spouse high; the refuter narrowed it to the label alone). "Each month" on the kept $84,000 re-reads the box's digits (`writeWorkingYearCostPeriod`, by design: one tap undoes a 12× misentry), and the row then reads **"$1,008,000 a year — the figure you kept"**, a sentence that is false (the household kept $84,000 a year). The rosy mirror (a kept monthly figure tapped to a year) dies on U5's carried stamp, which stales it with the lean.
- **Medium, the both-causes sentence drops the lean** (four seats; refuter: narrowed — the both half survives at P2, the state-only half dies, a state move can cut the tax on pay as easily as raise it). "If a working year now costs more than $84,000 a year" beside "$90,000 a year" of spending, which a working year almost never costs less than (`heldYesBelowQuote`'s own premise).
- **Medium, the Keep door after a state move names no tax** (refuter: narrowed — the lean is stated in the methodology; a wrong Keep leans rosy, PA's 3.07% on $150,000 is about $4,605 a year, inside the $6,000 here but not with a local wage tax). FIXED: the check paragraph reads the picture only (R6), with no partial-saver sentence; the partial saver's reply and its zero sit in the unanswered line, before the pick; each moved-line threshold is a share "of the $27,537"; "all of this", never "about", since the credit is all or nothing.
- **Fixed after walk 5, unwalked:** "tap" read as phone wording on the laptop and a re-tap could read as un-picking (now "choose “All of it” once more"); the middle branch's reason ("your answer still counts the $16,985 you said goes in") and the low branch's zero ("which adds none of it for now"); the statements sentence was a garden path and passed one calm year in a cycle with a car in it (now "against your account statements, not your budget: count what went in over a year, less anything you took back out, even for a car or a roof"); "the plan grows your savings by those itself" read as a promise of growth (now "already figures those in, in good years and bad"); the saved line now says why a later, larger left over adds only the answered figure.
- **HOLDS, filed (the card holds on them), re-found by all three walks:** b9-6's (a) the premium the left over leaves out (walks 3 to 5 name the mechanism: W equals the spending figure, confirmed in one tap, while the help says premiums paid while either works belong in W, and the $1,100 a month quote prices no year of `date`'s plan), (b) "savings" means three things (walk 4's hunter adds a 529, extra mortgage principal and an unlisted bank account), (c) "Spent" contradicts W, (d) the state-tax note reads as "already in W" (walk 4's copy-law seat: no line says to go back one question and add it), (e) the risen-quote clause. **New, holds-type:** (q) the re-entry gate names paychecks ("Markets and paychecks move") but shows no pay, no W and no left-over answer, so "Still about right" attests to balances alone while the date leans on all three (walk 3's hunter, spouse and copy-law seats).

Every walk re-found holds (a) and (d) as the remaining rosy reach.

It says I can quit in about 9 years at about 9-in-10 odds, marked provisional. Then it walks through a working year: $150,000 pay, minus payroll tax, federal tax, $20,000 saved and $84,000 to live on. The tone is calm and warm… I mostly trust it… Gray fine print that hands state tax back to me dents that. I feel encouraged, and I'm reaching for a bottom line that's cut off."* Readbacks matched the DOM.

**toneVerdict:** HARD-FLAG. One blocker and three holds-type findings fixed; walk 2 found the fixed check sentence still leaves a rosy reading, and five holds findings stay open.

**findings (verified by the chair against the bundle and the source):**
- **Holds (rosy), blocker, fixed.** The check sentence, "Choose “All of it” only if your savings grow by about this much each year, on top of what you put into savings", was passed by market growth alone: twelve seats on all seven targets, every refuter kept it (a $1.1M household, its $30,000 of investment income left in its accounts). A couple who spends the left over could tap "All of it" and be credited money never saved. FIXED: "…only if about this much of your pay ends up in your savings or investments each year, on top of what you already put into savings. Market gains, interest and dividends don’t count toward it." **Walk 2's hunter, still rosy (high):** it never states the $20,000 it sits on top of, or the total that answer implies ($36,985 a year on `date`), so "we stay on budget" can stand in for checking what really landed in the accounts. Prescription in TODO.
- **Holds (rosy), fixed.** The moved line on the gate, the Result and the seat said "adds at most the smaller of the two" with no figure and no object; a skim stopping at "$27,537 now" read that figure as credited (six seats), and the gate's "You chose “All of it”" clipped the label. FIXED: each names the smaller figure (`smallerFormatted`, a third slot argument), "of its left over to your savings", and the gate and Result say "You said all of it goes into your savings". Walk 2's copy-law seat read "to your savings" as the year's whole addition (the $20,000 deposits sit off the gate): "of its left over" was added after walk 2, unwalked.
- **Holds (rosy), fixed.** The moved methodology line dropped the saved line's caveat while still crediting (three seats): it now carries "It’s only as real as your figure for a working year: set too low, it adds money that isn’t really left over."
- **Medium, fixed.** The saved line capped every year at the first year's figure with no reason: it now says "the figure you chose “All of it” at" (walk 2: "the figure you said" named a figure never said; reworded, unwalked).
- **HOLDS, filed (the card holds on them):** (a) the money picture's left over carries no health premium where W is a confirmed spending quote and a spouse under 65 has stopped working (walk 2's hunter: `date`'s Sam, 60, a $1,100 a month quote), so "All of it" can credit up to the premium; (b) "savings" means three things, and a bank savings account fits both "All of it" (then grown at market returns) and "kept as cash" (walks 1 and 2); (c) "Spent" tells the tool a working year costs W plus the left over ($100,985 on `date`), and nothing questions the $84,000 (walks 1 and 2); (d) the unpriced state-tax note ("it belongs in your figure for a working year, below") reads as "already in W", so in a state that taxes pay the left over is too high (walk 1's hunter); (e) the stale clause after a RISEN quote (W's lean, then "none of the $16,985 a year you chose to save is added") can read as an offset that defuses "can look better than it really is"; no walk renders it (walk 1's hunter, `vault:datesavedstale`).
- **Filed, not holding:** the moved seat line's "Choose “All of it” again to use $27,537" reads as a refresh, never asks whether the raise is saved (walk 2, medium); "The tool doesn’t ask how much yet" (the how-much ask, D7); the moved note names no cause (pay rose) and "your first working year" no year; the stale clause's "you chose to save" names a figure the spouse can't place, and the waiting seat hides the stored answer; the reading order (walk 1's spouse seats: move the check sentence between the legend and the replies, which needs a hint slot in the shared `SegmentedControl`; kept above the legend for now, the W fork's pattern); the budget door's "Your answer uses about $78,000 a year" against the stale line's $84,000 (pre-existing); the waiting methodology line's passive "is checked". Methodology rows carry no dollars by design (walk 2's copy-law seat asked for them; kept).
- **Killed by refuters:** 15, among them "the question names no figure" (the figure line sits above it) and "the waiting line hides a reply" (W stale stores none by design).

**falsePassHunt:** attempted on every target in walk 1 and on the fixes in walk 2: walk 1 found the market-growth leak (the blocker), the state-tax reach and the rose-case clause; walk 2 found the unstated $20,000, the premium the left over leaves out, and the re-tap as a refresh.

**colorBlindCheck:** clean on every new surface: the chosen reply by border and weight (fill 1.19:1, never needed), the waiting seat by its icon, box and words, the moved and stale lines by words. Pre-existing nits only (the ladder's "Your date" dot and TwoFutures' order-only labels, filed earlier).

**comprehension:** walk 2's spouse: "does the $16,985 left after taxes, our $20,000 of saving and $84,000 of costs really end up in our savings?"; "our extra sits at the bank, which may count as cash: honestly I'd pick Some of it, or not sure"; "at most $16,985, and I can't tell whether that's on top of the $20,000".

Then the big dark button tells me to click "Still about right." That feels like it's inviting me to wave off a problem it just found."* (walk 1, `vault:wyclock` gate) *"…the note on the left that something was "updated since your save" leaves me unsure what changed."* (walk 3, `vault:datecoststale` gate) *"…It's honest: it says the answer uses exactly these numbers, and it flags one figure as out of date (a working year's cost, $84,000 against a newer $78,000). I trust it more for admitting that. But I didn't know what to do about that paragraph… neither button seems to be for it."* Readbacks matched the DOM on every frame; one crop was a capture defect (below).

**toneVerdict:** HARD-FLAG. Three holds-type fixes shipped and read clean; two holds findings are filed as builds, and the card holds on them.

**findings (verified by the chair against the bundle and the source):**
- **Holds (rosy), fixed.** `oopHelp`'s "Premiums are added on top by the tool" is false in every working year — `windowGate` (`healthcareStreams.ts`) zeroes the entered premium until the last worker stops — beside W's "count the health premiums you pay today" (four seats; the refuter confirmed it in source). FIXED: a date-route twin through `oopHelpKeyFor` (the `spendHelpKeyFor` doctrine): "The tool adds health premiums on top from the year you’ve both planned to stop working, and Medicare premiums for anyone on Medicare who has already stopped. Premiums paid while either of you still works belong in what a working year costs…". Walk 2's hunter caught the first draft's "once you’ve both stopped working" as a promise the engine breaks on a death path (the Tier 0 death-path entry, appended); the shipped words name the planned stop. Mutant-proven (the panel's call site reverted → the route arm red).
- **Holds (rosy), fixed.** The wyclock line's "the payroll tax and Social Security figures … have been updated" sat beside the one gate block not tagged "as saved", and five of six seats read "the tool refreshed our benefit" (survived refutation). FIXED: it names the two rules ("the Social Security and Medicare tax on pay, and the earnings limit that decides how much of an early Social Security benefit is held back") and "your own numbers below as you saved them". Walk 2's first look read it clean.
- **Holds (rosy, the mirror), fixed one-sided.** No stale sentence said which way an old W leans; where spending rose, the date reads unqualified (walk 1's hunter; the refuter traced the one-way direction in `taxOverlay.ts`: a higher W never improves the answer). FIXED: after a quote that rose, `slots.workingCostStaleLeansBetter` — "If a working year now costs more than $84,000 a year, this answer can look better than it really is." Walk 2's draft also said "can look worse" where spending fell: on `datecoststale` neither figure makes a year short and the curves are identical (walk 2's hunter, through the engine), and two seats read it as "the real answer is better" — WITHDRAWN. Walk 3's hunter asked "more than what?"; the slot now names the figure.
- **Medium, fixed.** KTD21's gate line quoted no figure, hedged "spending or state", and its "Worth a look" had nothing on the gate to look at (six seats; the refuter kept the gap, cut the harm — the answer repeats both figures). The gate now speaks the W step's own sentence (`workingYearCostStaleText` from the hydrated draft, `composeReentry`'s new required argument) under "Your answer still uses your last figure…", and, where the tab can save, "Either choice below leads to where you can change it." (three first looks asked which button fixes it; both do — the answer's door, and the walk, whose W step will not complete on a stale answer).
- **Capture defect, fixed.** `crop-w-row.png` painted the landing over its top third — a crop taller than the sheet's own scroller — so four seats read the row from copy.txt alone and the CVD seat could not screen it. `captureTallSheetRegion` (`e2e/caddie-walk.spec.ts`) crops a tall in-sheet region in segments, with CVD arms on the W row; walk 2's CVD seat read the segments clean.
- **HOLDS, filed — the card waits on them.** *The re-entry gate says "Markets and paychecks move" but shows no pay…* (walk 1's wyclock hunter, survived refutation; never filed before); *The gate's working-year lines can't say whether a change touches this household…* (the measured lean; the rules line's per-household gate; the stale face's two yeses; walk 3's double count — the years after work already run on $78,000).
- **Filed, not holding:** *Keeping a stale yes turns it into an entered figure, which never goes stale on spending again…* (walk 3; it re-opens ruling 34); *The spending help names only Medicare premiums to leave out…*. Appended: the later-years entry (the Medicare start's why), the caveats entry ("a little worse" sized at about $190,000 on `date`), the birth-month entry (whole-year benefit rows on the money picture), the investment-income entry (the $30,000 line's missing reason — a two-branch rewrite withdrawn: `date`'s savings are a 401(k) and a Roth, where such income is never taxed), the death-path entry.
- **Killed by refuters:** "Left over is spent, so W is about $17,000 low" (the household's own confirmed figure; the spec's NEGATIVE on defaulting a surplus to spent). **Dismissed by the chair:** the laptop verdict's below-fold disclaimer (the date route's ORDER contract, `e2e/vertical-fit.spec.ts`'s header — scroll sanctioned); the landing's "odds of what" (pre-existing; aria carries it).

**falsePassHunt:** attempted on every walk. Walk 1 found the out-of-pocket contradiction, the Social Security misread, the mirror lean and the gate's missing pay; walk 2 found the empty gloomy lean (measured) and the death-path promise; walk 3 found the double count, the Keep door's lost watch, and the unnamed comparison.

**colorBlindCheck:** clean — the gate notes set apart by position and ink; the W row by words, signs and weight (Left over larger and bold; the checked Yes by outline and weight, its tint fading in grayscale). Nit (pre-existing): the ladder's "Your date" fill matches the below-line dots in grayscale; the ring, label and aria settle it.

**comprehension:** walk 1's spouse: "can't tell if our Social Security changed"; "old $84,000 vs $78,000 — better or worse?". Walk 2's: "maybe better than it shows"; "I'd tap Something's changed"; "I'd tick Yes meaning keep the $84k" (the two yeses, filed).

**routedToOracle:** the stale W's effect, measured identical on `datecoststale` (walk 2's hunter, provisional tier, seed `0xbada55`) → the new Tier 1 entry's prescription (1); the gate's pay row → its own entry.

Trust: moderate to high, because it flags its own stale answer and admits state tax isn't priced. Each walk surfaced one more holds-type find, each fixed through the run's own figures and pinned: walk 1's "keep that tax inside this figure" (read as already counted), walk 2's federal-only tax line labelled as all income tax, and walk 2's first-look on the new Keep door's rosy direction.

**findings (verified by the chair against the bundle and the source):**
- **Holds-type (rosy), walk 1.** The unpriced state sentence's "keep that tax inside this figure": tax on pay is taken from pay, so no spending figure holds it, and "keep" read as already counted (two seats; the spouse seat's state-tax prong survived refutation). FIXED: the unpriced and PA sentences say "add" (ruling 35), pinned by a copy arm.
- **Holds-type (rosy), walk 2.** "Income tax on that income" on an unpriced household is federal only, but read as every income tax, so Left over looked like it came after a state tax the run never charged (three seats, survived refutation). FIXED through the engine (ruling 37): the ledger carries the run's own state slice (`taxOnOutsideIncomeState`), the label says "Federal and state…" only where it is above $0, and an unpriced or PA household is told its state's tax on pay went into W. The chair's first cut keyed that line to a zero slice; PA's real run (its tax on investment income priced, its tax on pay left to W) showed it wrong before it landed.
- **Flow (rosy, both ways).** Keeping a stale yes meant "a different figure", which clears it and unmounts the only place the old figure is named, while the easy Yes cut W by $6,000 (walk 1's hunter). FIXED: the Keep door on a held yes (ruling 34). Walk 2's first-look seat then saw the door as the stale note's only visible action, which would be a one-tap keep BELOW today's spending where spending rose. FIXED: the door is gated on the quote (shown only at or above it).
- **Medium, fixed.** The 111-word help paragraph (five of six walk-1 seats; b9-3's own prediction) is now two lists. "The tool starts charging Medicare only once work stops" named no one's work: "…someone's Medicare only once they stop working". The walk's unknown face printed the strip's two lines twice on one screen: it now points at the strip (`echoStrip`, ruling 36). The unpriced sentence's "for these years" implied later years are priced.
- **Filed, not built here (register):** the self-employed worker taxed at an employee's half (Tier 0, new); the later years' "what it holds back moves, or … begins" and "Medicare starts for Sam" with no dollars (Tier 1, new); the assumptions sheet showing no pay, no deposits and no coverage answer (Tier 1, new); the working-year caveats the household cannot sort into "us" and "not us" (Tier 1, new); door 1's era-less anchor, now contradicting the standing line on a stale W (appended to its entry); TwoFutures' order-only line binding (appended, CVD flag).
- **Killed by refuters (kept for the record):** "the bottom line hides Medicare" (both walks, a third time — the "Also paid from savings" group sits directly under it, R16); "Yes undercounts premiums" (both walks); the stale face's figure-less Yes (the note says the figures "now come to $78,000" and the quote under it repeats it).

**falsePassHunt:** attempted on both walks — walk 1 found the keep-door friction (the rosy Yes cut) and the federal-only tax line; walk 2 the tax line again (survived) and the stale Yes framing (refuted). Ruled out: the stale W's direction on this seed ($84,000 held is the gloomy side), leftover pay (never saved, said so), investment income (taxed, not counted as coming in), the Medicare-while-working stopgap (W keeps a pre-65 premium: gloomy).

Nit (pre-existing): the blocked alert's amber flattens in grayscale (the shared `FieldError`). Harness gap: the blocked and figure faces' CVD arms are above-fold frames, and the alert sits below the fold on the laptop, so no arm screens it.

Stumbles: the later years' either/or and the dollar-less Medicare start (both filed), "You said yes to $84,000" as a memory they don't hold.

**routedToOracle:** the tax line's scope → the engine's state slice (built, ruling 37, pinned in the engine and the UI); the four new register entries above.

## 2026-10-30 (b9-3) — the pay check's Phase B surfaces, pre-land (the uncommitted tree over `68557df9`) — `intake:paycheck` (the W step stale / blocked / figure-open faces; the money picture answered / short / unknown, across `datecoststale`, `dateshort`, `datesolo`) · `seed:datecoststale` (landing with the standing line, every door, the assumptions sheet's W row and its methodology frame), REAL+PHONE (walk `temp/caddie/b9-3-paycheck`, 4/4 in 1.6 m; panel `wf_b6e0a633-e5f`, 35 fresh Opus agents, 0 crashes)

**surface:** every new Phase B face — the W question (its quote, help, fork, figure and stale note), the year-0 money picture and its later years, the unknown face, the result's standing line, the panel's W row, the methodology row's W arm.

**firstImpression:** (intake, `w-step-stale`, REAL) *"A retirement-planning questionnaire, near its last step… It feels calm and cream-paper… I mostly trust it, but I feel a small unease: am I confirming spending alone, or spending plus premiums and taxes? And which number is the 7-year date using right now?"* (result landing) *"…I feel hopeful, then uneasy. Is this number real or not?"* Readback matched the DOM (no capture defect).

**toneVerdict:** HARD-FLAG — two surviving rosy holes in the W help's words (both lane `both`, the HOLDS rule), plus seven medium comprehension holes; every one fixed in words or wiring in the same tree, re-walk owed.

**findings (verified by the chair against the bundle and the source):**
- **HOLDS 1 (rosy).** `workingCostHelp` "plus any health premiums before anyone’s Medicare starts": on `dateshort` (Sam 66 on Medicare, Alex 63 working on her own plan) the literal reading counts no premium at all — Alex's plan drops out of every working year, and nothing else prices it. FIXED: "the health premiums you pay today for anyone not on Medicare yet".
- **HOLDS 2 (rosy).** The same help's "Leave out … Medicare premiums": the run starts a WORKER's Medicare only at the stop (`medicareOnsetForPerson`, `healthcareStreams.ts:125`), so a 65+ worker on Medicare was told to drop a cost nothing counts — the register's *Someone still working after 65…* said "no sentence covers it"; this one claimed it covered. FIXED: "Medicare premiums for anyone who has stopped working. If one of you is on Medicare while still working, count that premium here too. The tool starts charging Medicare only once work stops." (a stopgap until that build).
- **Medium.** "for a plan you buy yourself" beside `datesolo`'s refusal of exactly that coverage — scoped to "someone still working" (the fork's only priced self-bought plan). The picture's "Income tax on that income" silently carried the tax on $30,000 of investment income it never showed — a detail line now names the run's own figure. The result's standing line quoted no figure and blamed "spending or state" on a household with no state set — it now reads its lead, then the W step's own stale sentence (both figures, the cause that moved; one home, `workingYearCostStaleText`). The W row asked about a year beside "$6,500 a month" — the quote now carries "which is $78,000 a year". The methodology's K7 ("taxed by the state you said you'll live in…") showed where working years carry no state tax at all — gated to the readings the run taxes. The held-back sentence's garden path ("What’s paid after $2,760 is held back…") — "This is what’s paid after…". "No money picture to show **yet**" on a refused household — no "yet" where nothing can be added, and the strip's own closing line under the cannot-price name.
- **Killed by refuters (kept for the record):** "the bottom line hides Medicare" (the "Also paid from savings" group sits directly under it; R16 rules Medicare outside the sum); "Yes undercounts premiums" as a layout claim (the help sits between the quote and the fork, above the fold, on both viewports); the $1,100 marketplace quote as a working-year premium (the engine prices it at $0 while the plan at work covers both).

**falsePassHunt:** attempted — found HOLDS 1 and 2 (both above), killed the "stale W inflates the odds" reading on this seed ($84,000 held vs $78,000 now is the gloomy side).

**colorBlindCheck:** clean — selection by tint + border + weight + aria; the stale note by icon + box + words; Short vs Left over by word + sign. Nit: the blocked alert's salience flattens in grayscale (the shared `FieldError` style, pre-existing).

**comprehension:** the spouse read the answered household as "~$23k spare, fine", the short one as "short ~$17k, savings cover it", the unknown one as "no answer, now what?" — and stumbled on the month/year twins, whose figure "Yes" keeps, and "answer it above" after picking a different figure (the code review's B4, fixed in the same tree).

**routedToOracle:** none open — the investment-income tax detail reads the run's own carried figure (`buildDateInput`), and the code review (`wf_65e70286-868`) covered the wiring.

**prediction:** he stops on the W question's help as "too many things in one paragraph" (eight items) before any figure, asks "why is it asking me what I spend when I already told it?" on the quote, and reads the money picture's year 0 as the part he wanted all along ("show me how we got here"); he does NOT read the short year as fine once "Also paid from savings" is under it.

**disposition:** HARD-FLAG — HELD until the re-walk on the fixed tree reads both HOLDS findings clean (then PILOT-CLEARED; every new string ships flagged under the batched-oracle law). **→ PILOT-CLEARED 2026-10-30 (b9-5):** both HOLDS findings read clean on the fixed tree (`wf_a2c8ad47-cd5` — no seat found a rosy reading in either sentence, and every "Yes undercounts premiums" variant was refuted again); the re-walk's own finds are on the b9-5 card above.

## 2026-10-20/21 (b9-6) — the `working-year-tax` methodology row (U7 of the working-year tax floor), pre-land (the uncommitted tree over `4cdfc826`) — THREE walks, each `seed:retired` (all-retired) + `seed:date` (one still working): landing, every door, the assumptions panel AND its new `-methodology` frame (the sheet's own scroller brought to the end of the methodology rows — a harness gap closed this session in `e2e/caddie-walk.spec.ts`: no frame had ever painted those rows), REAL+PHONE — walk 1 the pilot draft (`temp/caddie/wyt-20261020`, panel `wf_48998b24-de1`, 28 seats), walk 2 his plain-words rewrite (`wyt-20261020-plain`, `wf_78c8c7fe-9e0`, 35), walk 3 the shipped words (`wyt-20261021-final`, `wf_9868160a-2c4`, 31)

**surface:** the sixth methodology row, "Tax while either of you is still working", three paragraphs — the premise and its one-way cost ("…the tool treats your pay as covering your living costs, the tax on your pay and investment income, and what you put into savings each year. It doesn’t check that your pay really covers all of that, so if it falls short, this answer looks better than it really is."), what savings still creates and how its tax is figured, the costliest-of-a-few-ways rule ("…Depending on your mix, your real tax could still be higher or lower."), and what is left out (a stopped member's investment income; his verbatim NIIT clause).

**firstImpression:** (walk 3, `seed:date`, REAL, the `-methodology` frame) *"A scrolled methodology/caveats modal over a retirement 'date' page (behind it: 'Your fu… 9 years…', 'about 9 of 10 odd…'). It lists what the tool assumes and which way each assumption pushes the answer. Tone: calm, candid, but a dense wall of run-on hedges and jargon. Trust: I trust the candor, but it lowers my trust in the headline, because it admits an unchecked assumption that makes the answer look better than reality. Feeling: uneasy and a bit buried. I came for reassurance and got a disclaimer list that hands me the job of judging it."* Readback: no dollar figure visible (the sheet covers it); "about 9 of 10 odd…" — matches the DOM.

**toneVerdict:** SOFT-FLAG — no rosier misreading of the row's OWN claims survives walk 3, but every walk read it as the panel's heaviest, densest row, and for an all-retired couple as a row about a life not theirs.

**findings (verified by the chair against the bundles):**
- **Walk 1 (the pilot draft):**
  - **The §7 clamp's one-way cost was never said.** Refuter-surviving on both targets: "counts your pay as covering your living costs" with no consequence for a household whose pay falls short. The accumulation decision record required that disclosure, and it never shipped. Now disclosed, and the gap is filed as a new Tier 0.
  - **The closing sentence presupposed an entry.** "the investment income you entered isn’t used for tax at all" told an all-retired couple about an entry they never made (rule 37), and readers took it for "our retirement dividends go untaxed". Cut.
  - **Garden paths and riddles.** "…or Medicare premiums — is taxed" garden-pathed (rule 5), and "left out" as the costliest reading read as a riddle. Fixed in the plain rewrite.
- **Walk 2 (his plain-words rewrite):**
  - **His chosen yield sentence read as a TAX RULE.** High on both targets, refuter-surviving. "interest and dividends aren’t taxed as they’re paid", with no "the tool" in it, teaches a false rule. A row of its own explaining the shortcut was "another example of we are way off course", so the sentence was pulled.
  - **The shortfall test was too narrow.** "against your spending" passed a household whose savings deposits made it short. Fixed to "all of that".
  - **"Money can still come out of savings, like a Roth conversion"** made a conversion sound spent. Fixed to "create taxable income".
  - **"A mix can cost more still"** read as a contradiction. Fixed to "higher or lower", true both ways and pinned by two REFERENT arms.
- **Walk 3 (the shipped words):**
  - **The highs mostly died.** The "investment income counts only for whoever is still working" tax-law reading was KILLED by three of four refuters, and so was the scope loss.
  - **SURVIVED, then fixed after the walk:** P2's "that tax" binding to the investment income (one of three refuters), and P1's "The tax on it… it’s paid" pronoun nit. Both were re-worded inside their string lines ("the tax on that income from savings", "That income is taxed… and the tax is paid"). The test pins both, and walk 3 did not re-read them.
  - **SURVIVED, routed:** the NIIT clause's "here" reads as the working years (his verbatim words, filed on the register's NIIT-direction entry); the row never tells an all-retired couple it isn't theirs; a stopped spouse's dividends carry no direction (the yield gap); and the tool has the numbers but doesn't check (the new Tier 0).

**falsePassHunt:** attempted: yes, every walk.
- Walk 1's hunter found the clamp's unstated optimism (SURVIVED, and disclosed).
- Walk 2's hunter found the yield sentence as tax law (SURVIVED, and pulled) and the narrowed self-test (fixed).
- Walk 3's hunter found the "that tax" binding (fixed after the walk) and the NIIT "here" (his words). Its other rosier attempts — "highest" as a ceiling, a stopped spouse's income, the shortfall test — all died.

**colorBlindCheck (flags only):** text only, with no chromatic meaning. The hierarchy holds in grayscale (heading 13.8:1, body 7.1:1), REAL and PHONE. Pre-existing on every methodology row: the row title is an aria paragraph, not a heading.

**comprehension (the spouse):**
- Walk 3, retired: "On track, 9 of 10, close to the line… Ending the panel on this row left me unsure if any of it was about us."
- Walk 1, working: "we look okay (2035, about 9 of 10). Next I'd ask Alex whether one paycheck covers $7,000 a month."

**routedToOracle:**
- the NEW Tier 0, *A working year never checks the household's pay against its spending…* — a ⚑ HOLDS entry. The row discloses it, but the landing's date reads early for a short household, with no flag on it;
- the existing Tier 0, *The taxable account's yield never reaches a year's tax base…* — its interim sentence was withdrawn;
- the NIIT "here" scope, his words, on the register's NIIT-direction entry.

**prediction:** He stops on "It doesn’t check that your pay really covers all of that" — not as a words problem but as the off-course question he already raised in chat ("why the fuck not? what are we building???", E20), and reads the row as more method than a couple needs. If he reads the NIIT line, he asks whether "here" means only while working.

**disposition:**
- **the change PILOT-CLEARED.** Walk 3 left no rosier misreading of the row's own claims, and its two surviving referent findings were fixed inside their lines before land.
- **the surface HARD-FLAG — HELD FOR THE ORACLE** on two ⚑ HOLDS entries this change neither created nor worsened: the pay-check gap (since the June clamp) and the yield gap (since the engine's start).
- **PARKED-FOR-BRIGGSY:** his OFF-COURSE discussion ("I really think we're off course here. A discussion for another day"), which owns the static-versus-route-true row, the yield disclosure and the pay check's remedy; and the NIIT "here", which is his words.

Most refuters KILLED it on context (the next paragraph's "extra-coverage premiums"); the `pa` and `datenc` spouse findings SURVIVED at medium. → his b9-2 re-ruling ("Medicare’s premiums … a drug plan, a supplement plan …").
- **The b9-2 line — every rosier attempt KILLED (walk 2).** The pa hunter: "copays read as added on top (the b9-1 regression) dies on 'premiums'"; "a drug plan" no longer reads as prescriptions; "Those are the Medicare pieces" and the Tail's "extra-coverage premiums" both bind. - **The trim note — clean.** No "never" reading survives on any seat, REAL or PHONE, fold closed or open; "rather than" tints toward cut-now advice (spouse, calm, hunter — tone, nit to medium, never rosier). **routedToOracle:** none (no holds finding on the changed words; the MA misreading was a words question — the engine fact, premiums on top and out-of-pocket inside the spending, is settled — answered by his re-ruling).

## 2026-10-16 — THE ONE-FIGURE MEDICARE CARD'S FIRST-YEAR FRAME + BRIGGSY'S TREND CLAUSE ON THE HEALTHCARE SHEET, pre-land (the uncommitted tree over `cd4af81f`) — `seed:healthss`, landing + every door (the Healthcare sheet as figured + its preview), REAL+PHONE (walk 1 `temp/caddie/medicare-first-year-1015` — the dated "start at" frame alone; walk 2 `temp/caddie/medicare-trend-1016` — with his clause; each 2/2 in ~29 s; six lenses + ten refuters per walk, fresh Opus agents, panels `wf_b9b5c579-ee0` and `wf_345d9c50-32d`; the card scrolled into frame by `e2e/held/medicare-cards-look.spec.ts` as a `door-5-…-scrolled-premium` state at both viewports)

**surface:** the Part B card's one-figure arm for two or more — eyebrow "Part B premiums and any surcharge while you’re both on it" over "~$5,200 a year"; "About three years from now, around 2029, while you’re both on Medicare, Part B premiums and any income surcharge start at about ~$5,200 a year for your household." / "That would be the base rate, with no income surcharge on the middle-of-the-road path." / (walk 2) "Even in today’s dollars, each person’s Part B premium is likely to keep rising." / the drug-and-supplement carve-out. Was: "In your plan’s Medicare years, premiums could run about ~$5,200 a year for your household — the base rate, …" under "Part B premiums and any surcharge".

**firstImpression:** (walk 2's cold seat, `healthss` landing, REAL) *"A retirement-plan result page for a couple who are both 62. It tells me they're fine: "More than covered", better than 9 in 10 futures work, there's room to spend more than $5,000 a month, and the plan still holds if one of them is left alone. The tone is calm, plain and warm, like a quiet advisor rather than a dashboard. The caveats are honest (state tax isn't priced yet, not advice). I mostly trust it. It made me feel relieved and unhurried. The chart agrees: the middle line drifts up from $2M."* Readback matched `copy.txt` ("more than $5,000 a month / at about $7,100 a month"; "better than 9 in 10 futures your plan covers") — no capture defect. The cold seat reads the landing by design; the card lives on door 5.

**toneVerdict:** walk 1 — HARD-FLAG (⚑ holds: the dated "start at" alone read as WHEN, not a rise; the card still priced the era at its cheapest year in the reader's head). Walk 2 — READS-CLEAN on the change: the "every year" reading and the basis finding are dead; SOFT-FLAG on one residual (the rise is unsized — his words) and on pre-existing sheet findings.

**findings (verified by the chair against both bundles; 20 refuters):**
- **WALK 1, CREATED-THEN-FIXED — the trend.** "start at about" after "around 2029" reads as onset (spouse, copy-law, calm, hunter — all high; three of four refuters SURVIVED it, the fourth: "the register leaves those words to Briggsy"). The pilot's no-trend-line lean (the no-era precedent) was wrong. FIXED by his clause (asked in-session; "I like the reco wording", then "yep that was it").
- **WALK 1 → 2 — the dollar basis.** "around 2029 … ~$5,200" with no basis invites the 2029 bill (~$5,724, V.E2 $238.50 × 24; copy-law + hunter, refuters SURVIVED at medium in walk 1). Walk 2: KILLED by both refuters — his clause says "today's dollars" two lines under the figure. Residual "implied, not stated": low, the ruled point.
- **WALK 2, SURVIVED → NEW TIER 1 (words his) — the rise has no size.** copy-law and calm at high; both refuters SURVIVED it at MEDIUM ("Briggsy approved this figure-less wording … adding a figure is his call"). The register's *The Part B trend is spoken with a direction and no size…*.
- **WALK 2, REFUTED:** "budget ~$5,200 for life" (spouse, high — killed on the dated "start at" + the clause), the eyebrow alone claiming the era (copy-law + hunter, high — killed: three signals sit beside it in the same frame), "keep rising never says the plan counts it" (copy-law, calm, hunter, medium — killed: the held-flat line sits ~30 lines down, scoped to the extras; a misread errs cautious), "$9,700 then $5,200 reads as a drop" (spouse, high — killed again under the 2026-09-14 eyebrow ruling). Walk 1 also REFUTED the step card's room as rosy (the engine's IRMAA MAGI makes it ~$2–3k conservative).
- **SEEN AGAIN, already filed:** "the lines below" pointing ~650 CSS px down to per-person rows under a third name (the Tier 2 held-flat-walk entry); the Part B card never saying who adds it (door 6's helper; a misread errs cautious); the landing's state-tax caveat, the "eight in ten band / better than 9 in 10" pair, the blank band above the extras, the bare "28¢", the "about ~" double hedge. Nits logged: the step card's "+~$3,300" is one year's price (the ~2031 bill) — the same one-year shape, on the step card's open "a year" entry; "~$232,900" undated beside today's published $218,000.
- **BUILD REVIEW (code lane, `wf_0f6a7da2-fb1`), not a seat:** the surcharged first year's "start at" reads as a floor — filed Tier 1 (pessimistic, pre-existing); the clause is withheld there.

**falsePassHunt:** attempted: yes, at depth on both walks (walk 2's hunter priced Part B from V.E2 by hand: $5,208 = $238.50 deflated 3.2 %/yr × 3 × 24, nominal $5,724; ~$7,700 by 2047 via the real escalator). SURVIVING: the rise's size — read as "a bit" (medium, his words).

The scrolled state has no CVD arm or aria snapshot (the pilot's frames — the harness gap); the door-5 CVD arms stop above the card. Next: "ask my spouse what our real drug and supplement plans cost." Stumbles: "how much does it rise?" (the Tier 1), "the lines below" (filed), the eyebrow's "it".

**routedToOracle:** the unsized rise (NEW Tier 1, words his); the surcharged first year's floor (NEW Tier 1, from the build review); the one-figure card's cheapest-year Tier 0 CLOSED (the register's ✅ entry).

- **Seen again, stapled:** the room read as the discount (the first-look seat — a third cold reader); the ACA card's figure with no era named (*A wide-gap household's solo Medicare era…*); the doubled hedge "about ~$" and the unhedged "22¢" headline (nits, logged).
