Sobering, but it gives me something to do… a bit deflated, then oriented."* (readbacks matched `copy.txt`: $6,500 a month; 9 of 10 / 6 of 10.)

**toneVerdict:** SOFT-FLAG on this change (two of its strings, both FIXED before land, below); HARD-FLAG on PRE-EXISTING surfaces (the holds rule — filed, none created by this change).

**findings (verified by the chair against the bundle; 16 refuters, 13 survived, 3 killed):**
- **FIXED — the survivor-ratio clause read as a research finding** (lane both; refuter R16 survived): the draft's passive *"Any out-of-pocket health costs you entered are held whole, not scaled by this share"*, directly after *"Research on surviving spouses lands around three-quarters…"*, read as research — the council's honesty condition forbids exactly that, and the dialog names its sibling leans ("deliberately conservative default"). Now: *"The tool deliberately keeps any out-of-pocket health costs you entered whole rather than scaling them by this share — the cautious choice."* Pinned (`copyGuard.test.ts`, the drift-pin; a planted revert reds it).
- **FIXED — `oopHelp` handed a no-HSA household a phantom account** (tone, medium, spouse seat): *"The tool uses this to size your HSA's tax-free draw"* on a household holding brokerage / pre-tax / Roth only. Now conditional — *"If you have an HSA, the tool uses this to size its tax-free draw. And if one of you is on your own later, the tool keeps this amount whole rather than scaling it down — a deliberately cautious choice."*
- **PRE-EXISTING, ⚑ HOLDS — the survivor card never states the spending it assumes** (both, high; R13 / R15 / R19 / R24 survived): "9 of 10 futures your plan still covers you" names only the headwinds; it assumes the survivor spends ~$59,500 a year (~$4,960 a month on `health`) — a scared spouse reads "covered at our $6,500". Filed Tier 1 (register *The survivor card's "still covers you" never says what spending it assumes…*).
- **PRE-EXISTING, ⚑ HOLDS — the step-down is a steady-state figure** (both, high; R23 / R26 survived): `healthgap`'s "steps down about $1,930" holds only once both benefits would pay; for a 61/40 couple a death before the younger turns 60 leaves NO survivor benefit for years (`simulate.ts`'s tStar = max(first death, later claim)). Stapled to the register's *The survivor step-down attribution…* entry.
- **PRE-EXISTING — the trim sentence's third line under an Off-track verdict** (both; refuters SPLIT — R21 survived, R20 killed it): "Above that, it starts to sit close to the line" reuses the room sentence's tail, so a household at $6,500 can read itself as near the line, not off track. Filed Tier 1 (register *The trim sentence ends "Above that, it starts to sit close to the line"…*).
- **PRE-EXISTING — "The few costs this tool leaves out are listed…" claims completeness** (both; R12 / R14 survived): the list omits long-term care beside a ~$456,200 lifetime health total. Stapled to *Long-term care is neither modeled nor listed as left out*.
- Killed: R17 (the mechanism IS one tap away on door 6), R18 (the $7,000 room is survivor-gated by `solveSpend`, so reading it as holding alone is TRUE), R20.

**falsePassHunt:** attempted: yes; rosier readings FOUND — all on pre-existing surfaces (the survivor card's unstated spending basis; the steady-state step-down; the completeness claim), none created by this change. On the change itself: `healthgap`'s Off-track verdict read honest, calm, not alarmist (spouse + calm seats); the trim figure is the spend lane's solved, round-tripped value.

**colorBlindCheck (flags only):** verdicts carry an arrow glyph + word + count on household and survivor cards; band tiers split by lightness in grayscale / deutan / protan; selected controls carry fill + heavy border + weight + aria-checked. No color-only state found.

The health-sheet preview's sits over a real benefit (fine).

**prediction:** He clears the change (the Off-track `healthgap` reads as earned, the copy as plain); if he flags anything it is the survivor card not saying what spending it assumes — pre-existing, now filed Tier 1.

**disposition:** **the change PILOT-CLEARED** after its two copy fixes (both his words, drafted — ⚑ yours-to-close per the council); **the surface HARD-FLAG — HELD FOR THE ORACLE** on the two pre-existing ⚑ holds findings (survivor spending basis; steady-state step-down), which this change neither created nor worsens (it RAISES survivor spending, the cautious direction). ## 2026-09-27 — THE MEDICARE STEP CARD'S MOVED FIGURES (the crossing priced at the bill year's scales, `2a0f6d80`) — `seed:healthnc` door 5 (health sheet), REAL+PHONE; `seed:retired` walked but offers no health door (bundles `temp/caddie/2026-09-27-stepcard`, `-scope`, `-scope2`; walks 4/4 + 2/2 + 2/2, ~30 s each)

**Scope, and why the panel was words-only.** The figures moved $1,100 → $1,700 each ($2,300 → $3,400 for two; `retired`'s unrendered card $2,300 → $2,400); the copy did not. The step figure wears tabular numerals (`base.css`'s figure law, re-declared on `.hs-fact__figure`) at equal glyph counts, so the pixels are layout-identical by construction — and no walk pixel reaches the card anyway (the sheet scrolls inside its dialog; the register's Tier 4 door-walk gap). Seated: the spouse walker, the copy-law finder → a refuter, the calm/honesty reader, the false-pass hunter (Opus, fresh, de-authored; corpus with E17 + the newly folded E18 and E17's re-read). NOT seated, reason logged: the first-look reader and the CVD screener (no pixel of the card; no color or layout moved).

**toneVerdict: SOFT-FLAG → the moved figures PILOT-CLEARED; one comprehension survivor's WORDS PARKED-FOR-BRIGGSY.**

**Card A — the surcharge's scope is unnamed, and the TRUE price exposes it (lane both, high; newly visible).** Two seats independently read "+~$1,700 each" against the ~$2,900-a-person premium as too big (~59 % vs their ~45 % from 2026-sized Part D) and suspected a bug; the chair verified the figure against the engine (2032: Part B ~$1,154 + Part D ~$533 — the IRA §11201 2030 reset roughly triples tier-1 Part D, Trustees V.E4) — correct, and the premium card is on the SAME 2032 clock. The label is what's missing: the premium card says drug PLANS are not counted, the step card never says its surcharge covers the drug-plan surcharge. Two scope clauses built red-first and re-read cold: *"in Part B and drug-plan surcharges"* collided with the plans-not-counted line (high); *"in income surcharges on Part B and on your drug plan"* dropped the collision to medium but drew a NEW rosy survivor from the hunter (a $0 drug plan reads as escaping the Part D share). Rosy outranks confusing, so the chair REVERTED to the shipped wording and PARKED the words (the thrash limit: one refinement) — register *The step card never says what its surcharge covers…*, the three drafts and their misreads as the brief.

**Card D — the step card's room covers the one-enrollee years, where the Marketplace cliff binds (lane correctness, high; pre-existing; rosy).** *"By those Medicare years … roughly ~$179,600 under it"* reads for 2030–31, when the younger spouse is still on the discounted plan with ~$18,600 of room. Filed Tier 1 beside the look-back headroom entry (same sentence).

**Filed, tone, medium (pre-existing):** "a year" reads as a standing cost (scary direction; the refuter trimmed); the survivor sentence quotes no dollar line (three seats). **Killed by the refuter:** the hypothetical-as-charge headline, "conversion room reads tax-free", "The next step" implying a current step. **Nits noted, not filed:** the sheet-wide "about ~$" doubled hedge (a standing grammar choice), three "it"s.

- **falsePassHunt:** attempted twice on the shipped copy (Survivor 1 = Card D; Survivor 2 killed by the refuter) and once on each draft (draft 2's $0-plan survivor is why it did not ship).
- **colorBlindCheck:** not seated — no color or layout moved (flags only; this lane never passes anything).
- **comprehension:** the spouse walker got the step, the room and the per-person/household pair right; stumbled on the figure's size (Card A) and on which years "those Medicare years" means (Card D / the look-back entry).
- **routedToOracle:** none — the figure's correctness was verified by the chair and is pinned red-first (`healthSheetSeedGate`'s Trustees-row derivation).

- **disposition:** PILOT-CLEARED (the moved figures) · PARKED-FOR-BRIGGSY (Card A's words — pilot doubt after three drafts).

## 2026-09-25 — BRIGGSY's N=1 COLD READ of Card 3's sentence — `seed:retired` `landing-worsened` + `panel-worsened`, REAL+PHONE (walk `temp/caddie/card3-cold`, 2/2 in 23.5 s; frames shown cold, no ★ named)

**Supersedes the 2026-09-17 card's disposition on one point: severity.** The sentence on the frame: *"Spending about $2,800 a month instead of $10,000 — about $7,200 less — would move it toward steadier ground."* Asked open ("what is the screen telling this couple?"): **"This couple is fucked — but that's ok (from the software's perspective — it's giving them honesty)."** Asked to put the line under "1 of 10" in his own words: **"spend 2800 a month instead of 10k and they'd be ok."** Asked how ok, and for how long: **"that's what the tool tells us, no?"**

**What it means.** The headline reads exactly as designed (honest, not doom). The trim clause does NOT: the target reader converts "toward steadier ground" into ARRIVAL ("they'd be ok") and believes the tool has told him both the odds and the duration. It has told him neither — and cannot: the figure is `confidence.ts` `buildDollar`'s coarse proxy (−spend × the gap below on-track, "NOT a solve"); the engine never ran the plan at $2,800. That is the implied-sufficiency reading the 2026-09-17 card's survivor (1) named and the chair filed at Tier 2 / PILOT-CLEARED. The survivor was right; **the chair's severity was wrong** — "toward" as direction held on eight machine seats and failed on the one human who matters. **Disposition:** the register entry *The trim clause's frame — a gauge, not a solve* moves to **Tier 0** (Briggsy's ruling, 2026-09-25: "fuck yeah, go"), the fix goes to a council, and any new words are Caddie-read on this frame before they ship. Taste corpus: rule 15 widened + exemplar E17.

**The fix, re-read at his seat the same night (2026-09-26 early; frames `temp/caddie/trim-figureless`, walk 2/2 in 23.3 s):** the council's figure-less line (`wf_8c2ece49-79a`) — *"Spending less than $10,000 a month would let your plan cover more futures. This answer doesn't work out how much less."* — shown cold at REAL + PHONE with the same three questions. His verbatim: **"looks good"** — a sign-off of the WORDS; the comprehension questions (line 3 in his words · how ok / how long · honest vs dodging) went unanswered, so the entry's close condition (read as neither "they'd be ok" nor a shrug) is not yet witnessed. Asked once more ("what is the line under '1 of 10' telling this couple?"): **"yeah that looks good"** — CLOSED on his ruling; recorded as two sign-offs, no restatement.

## 2026-09-17 — CARD 3's SENTENCE, read on the frame that filed it — `seed:retired` `landing-worsened` + `panel-worsened`, REAL+PHONE (the fourth Sonnet-5 dual-panel A/B walk: `wf_825c66dc-58e`, 28 agents, 0 crashes, 5.9 m; bundle `temp/caddie/2026-09-17-card3`, walk 2/2 in 23 s)

**Why it existed.** The 2026-09-11 walk's top calm-but-wrong (its Card 3, BLOCKER): *"About $7,500 a month less would move it toward steadier ground."* on a household whose $10,000 sat 1,800 px down the assumptions sheet. Briggsy's 2026-09-13 grant discharged the words to the pilot with "Caddie-read before ship"; the register's Tier 0 entry closes only on this read. Built 2026-09-17 (b9-1, the register entry carries the plumbing): *"Spending about $2,500 a month instead of $10,000 — about $7,500 less — would move it toward steadier ground."* The focus: does a cold reader now see the size of the ask without opening anything; do the three figures read as one statement; does "toward" still read as direction; is the aside legible at both wraps; and the hunt — any reading of it as a tune-up, a promise, or the tool's own recommendation.

**The focus, answered — CLEARED on every seat of both panels.** Both endpoints ride in-sentence and 2,500 + 7,500 = 10,000 reconciles on the hero and the panel echo at REAL and PHONE (Opus calm: "the Card-3 fix lands its own goal … in the panel the echo sits beside the literal 10,000 field"; Sonnet calm: "prior BLOCKER resolved"); "toward" reads as direction, never arrival, on all eight perception seats and the hunter; two-line wrap at 1536, three-line at 390, the aside intact, nothing clipped, the hero above the fold in both `fold.json`s; the sentence survives colour loss on both panels (glyph + word carry the verdict; the clause is plain paragraph text in the a11y tree). The tune-up reading is DEAD — the refuters killed it on the three adjacent rendered lines ("Off track", "1 of 10 futures your plan covers", the fan hitting $0) and on the in-sentence delta ("nothing to subtract"); the recommendation reading was killed on the subjunctive + the modeled frame + the in-frame not-advice caveat.

**What survived the refuters — four, every one a property the OLD sentence had too (the delta-only form hid them; the endpoints expose them).** (1) **The payoff is unpriced** (Opus spouse, high): the ask is quoted to the dollar and what it buys is a direction with no odds after it; the product prices moves elsewhere (door 3's "about 9 of 10 either way"), so silence on its biggest ask reads as implied sufficiency. The refuter's own caution stands with it: the fix is NOT a new odds number — the trim is a coarse proxy, not a solve. (2) **No duration** (the hunter, high): "instead of" implies the standing figure, but no time word says the model holds the cut for the whole plan; "a few years" is the rosier walk-away. (3) **No lever framing** (Sonnet copy-law, medium): under "Off track / 1 of 10" the cut is the only actionable sentence, and on the phone the strategy door is two screens down. (4) **The depletion line is sr-only** (Opus spouse, high; the refuter confirmed the source and narrowed it) — REFUTED AT THE CHAIR: that routing is the ratified O3 decision (council 2026-07-10; `architecture.md` §12 names the one `.sr-only`), a locked decision this read cannot reopen; recorded here so no seat re-files it as new. **Routed:** (1)–(3) are ONE register entry, Tier 2, council — *The trim clause's frame — a gauge, not a solve* — because each is a framing question about hero line 3's STATUS, bigger than Card 3's words, and the honest fix for (1) is a status word, never an odds figure the engine has not computed. **Two more from seats the refuters never saw, verified by the chair at source:** the Opus CVD seat's **echo ink inversion** — the panel echo paints the clause in `--ink-muted` (`.ap-echo__dollar`) under a full-ink subordinate line, while the hero's action-first rule lifts the same sentence to full ink (`confidence.css`); measured on `crop-echo.png` (ask ~83 luminance, the line beneath ~39.5) — filed Tier 2, pilot, S (*The assumptions echo paints the trim clause in MUTED ink…*); and its **harness note** — no CVD arm of `panel-worsened` contains the echo (the arms are scrolled to the fields) — widened onto the Tier 4 scroll-and-clip entry. The Opus calm seat's "$2,500 arrives unattributed / 'it' has no antecedent" (medium, unrefuted) and the Sonnet spouse's "flat declarative, no 'one option' framing" (killed by the refuter on the modeled frame) both ride the frame entry as evidence. **Disposition: PILOT-CLEARED** — the Tier 0 defect is gone on every seat; the residuals are filed, not shipped-around.

**Killed, and why (so nobody re-files):** the "$7,500 reads as the target" glance-misread — the phone wrap puts "$2,500 a month" in the sentence-opening clause with the verb and "less" renders adjacent to $7,500 on both viewports (the finding cropped its referent clause out); "the proportion is missing" — a preference for "three-quarters" over dollars, a rewrite not a defect, and the size is stated in-sentence; the em-dash-aside shape (Opus copy-law, Sonnet copy-law, the hunter) — tone-only, legible at both wraps; "$10,000 wears no unit" — parallelism with "a month" carries it; the panel-echo "no odds" duplicate; and the Sonnet nit that the phone wrap splits the pair — it splits at "instead of", the pair's own hinge.

**THE A/B DIFF (the tape scores both panels).** Findings per seat, Opus vs Sonnet: spouse 5 / 3 · copy-law 5 / 3 · calm 4 / 1 · cvd 4 / 0. Refuter survival: Opus 2 of 11, Sonnet 1 of 4, the hunter 1 of 2. **Sonnet MISSED both Opus survivors** — the unpriced payoff and the sr-only depletion line — and its first-look read the $0-crossing chart as "scarier than intended" (the rosier direction of the same fact Opus called invisible). Its one survivor (the lever framing) Opus's hunter also filed at medium (never sent to a refuter); the Sonnet seat's copy survived on the phone's two-screen distance to the door, and the chair keeps that reading. **Verdict on the flip: NOT clean — the fourth walk in a row. Perception seats stay Opus.**

**Measured, not a defect:** the worsened landing at 1536×791 was 830 px tall on the BASE tree (the doors' padding already 39 over the frame) and is 858 with the second line; the door links sit at y 759, above the fold; the one-frame law gates resting states, and `retired` rests on-track with a one-line clause.

**prediction (the tape's scoring hook, in his vocabulary):** ★1 he reads the new line cold and does NOT ask "less than what" — he says some version of "got it, three-quarters" (a HIT is the absence of the 2026-09-11 prediction's "we spend ten grand?"); ★2 he asks "does 2,500 actually fix it?" (the unpriced payoff, in his words); ★3 he does not ask "for how long?" (the hunter's duration read is a FALSE-FLAG if so); ★4 on the phone he never reaches the strategy door from this line and says the page "just tells me to cut" (the lever framing).

## 2026-09-14 — THE TWO MEDICARE CARDS, read together — `seed:healthnc` · `seed:healthgap`, REAL+PHONE (the third Sonnet-5 dual-panel A/B walk: `wf_ae8a3b48-53d`, 62 agents, 1 crash — the Sonnet CVD seat on healthgap died on a 201-character observation against the schema's 200 cap; 18 m; bundle `temp/caddie/2026-09-14-medicare`, walk 4/4 in 33 s; plus the chair's scroll-and-clip LOOK frames, `temp/look/2026-09-14-{before,after}/`)

Every seat judged the two cards from `copy.txt` / `dialog.txt` / `aria.yaml` — the WORDS, the skill's ground truth — and said so in its observations; the LOOK went unjudged by the panel. The chair made the frames the walk could not (`temp/look/medicare.look.ts`: the dialog element screenshotted at his seat + phone with the sheet scrolled to each card, and once with the scroller's max-height lifted) and read them: pure type hierarchy (eyebrow → hero → body), nothing clipped or overprinted inside the scrolled frame, the tag's 0.4em dash margin breathes on the phone. **Routed: register Tier 4 (new) — the door walk needs a per-region scroll-and-clip capture for any sheet whose scrollHeight exceeds its frame** (the 2026-09-13 actions-row landmine, now the whole read).

**The focus, answered.** The eyebrow does the era work (calm, both panels: "the per-arm eyebrow does real era work"; the hunter: "every figure hedged, no casino tells"); the reading order of lines 2–3 held (the loud era first, the on-ramp second — the sonnet calm seat on healthgap called the near era "relegated", the refuter narrowed it to the undated hero and it rides Card 2's fix); what moved was the WORDS, on two of the five lines and on the step card:

**Card 1 — THE STEP CARD'S HERO IS PER-PERSON UNDER A HOUSEHOLD ERA: HARD-FLAG, lane `both`, HIGH — BUILT this session.** Six seats across both panels and both seeds plus the hunter on each. On healthgap four survived; on healthnc the refuters KILLED it on "×1 IS the household figure at the anchor year" — true, and beside the point the survivors made: the premium card has just established "while you're both on it", and a reader binds the step to that era. Verified in the frames: "+~$1,100 a year" bare, in the big type, directly under "~$5,800 a year for your household"; "for each of you" is the tail of a four-line body. The sheet's own convention convicts it (the hunter, both seeds): the one non-household hero already declares its unit IN the hero — "22¢ on each dollar converted". Built: the each-of-you hero wears its unit (*"+~$1,100 a year each"*, `healthFigStepAddEach`), and its sentence carries BOTH counts with the era in the eyebrow's own words — *"Crossing it could add about ~$1,100 a year for each of you on Medicare. While you're both on it, that's about ~$2,300 a year."* — the ×2 exact at one tier, formatted ONCE from the unrounded product (2 × the rounded '1,100' reads $2,200 — the panel's own number — $100 low); the two-of-you arm and `retired` byte-untouched. **Register Tier 0 entry CLOSED.**

**Card 2 — "THE FIRST 21 YEARS" OF WHAT: HARD-FLAG, lane `both`, HIGH (a Sonnet copy-law seat at BLOCKER) — BUILT.** Five seats across both panels on healthgap, every refuter survived: neither era named WHEN it starts, so read from today — the natural read — the ~$2,700 on-ramp figure lands on the household's first four years, which the ACA card above prices at ~$9,000 (rosier by ~$6,300 a year for those years; the wire says anchor year 5, era year 26). The only rendered origin was an age tick on the band behind the modal's scrim. Built: both lines open with their distance from the wire's own `yearsFromNow` (never an age — the no-proxy law): *"From about 26 years out, while you're both on Medicare, Part B premiums and any income surcharge start at about ~$8,100 a year for your household."* / *"Before that, from about five years out and for about 21 years, only one of you is on Medicare and the premiums start at about ~$2,700 a year."* — "Before that" because the loud era is quoted first and the on-ramp comes earlier in time; "the premiums" for a "they" whose antecedent sat a sentence away (sonnet calm, nit); "a year" on every figure (sonnet calm read the bare "~$2,700." as a two-year total — medium, refuter-unchecked, chair-verified in `copy.txt`); "starting now" when the anchor is today, "a year out" at one. **Register: filed and CLOSED in one motion.** Six mutants planted, each predicted, each red where predicted, the tree restored byte-exact; the after-frames read at his seat + phone.

**Card 3 — THE EYEBROW OVER-CLAIMS: "Medicare premiums while you're both on it" over a Part-B-scoped hero — HARD-FLAG, lane `both`, HIGH; PARKED-FOR-BRIGGSY (the register's extras framing fork — the words are his either way).** The walk's strongest convergence inside the focus: the Opus spouse, calm and copy-law seats and the hunter on BOTH seeds filed it. ## 2026-09-13 — THE TWO SIBLING SHEETS' BLOCKED APPLY — `seed:retired` (the Roth door) · `seed:healthnc` (the health door), REAL+PHONE, plus the scroll-and-clip LOOK frames (bundle `temp/caddie/2026-09-13-sheets`; walk 4/4; the panel ran TWICE — the session limit killed 10 of 12 seats mid-read, resumed after the 7:20 pm reset with the two first-look seats replayed from cache: 35 agents, 0 crashes, 16 m)

**Why it existed.** The Card 14 review found the two lever sheets opening with their Apply muted by tint alone, no reason at rest, and the health sheet's blocked press announcing "Working out both futures…" over a preview that had withdrawn to idle. Built the same evening (register: *The two sibling sheets' blocked Apply*, CLOSED); this walk is the pre-ship read of the SENTENCE and the LOOK on both sheets. The chair verified every surviving finding against the bundle and, where a mechanism is named, against source.

**The focus, answered — READS-CLEAN on the sentence and on the look, from every seat that judged them.** Roth: *"Set a yearly amount to add this to your plan."* is TRUE on arrival (the amount blank, year and length pre-filled), names the dialog's own precondition and never the household's money, calm, one line at 1536 AND at 390 (spouse, copy-law, calm, hunter — independently). Health: *"Your plan is already figured this way — pick the other rule to compare."* the same, two lines on the phone; the two sheets speak one dialect. The LOOK: blocked vs live is a luminance INVERSION, not a tint — the filled slab (11.4:1 label) against paper + a 1 px dashed `--line-control` hairline (3.60:1) + `--ink-muted` (7.14:1); holds in grayscale / protanopia / deuteranopia at both seats (cvd, flags-only lane). Source-confirmed by the hunter: the seen and the spoken sentence are ONE key per face (applied-passed > typed-past > missing field); a blocked press never speaks the pending line over a dead preview; hover is pixel-identical to rest (no darken), the cursor stays a pointer, the press answers. **The false-pass hunt** attempted and killed: "add this to your plan" as the tool executing the conversion (the sheet's what-if vocabulary kills it); a hidden second gate behind the amount sentence (2026 / 5 are pre-filled; `rothPlanMissingField` and `complete()` read the same three arms); the typed-past face "missing" its reason (the alert and the button share one frame in both look frames); "$18,600 of room", the IRMAA headroom, the ~$318,600 vs ~$332,800 preview (all quoted with both endpoints); "Working out both futures…" on the picked face (a TRUE pending). Chair-killed besides: the "yearly" vs "dollars a year" label unevenness (only one of the three sentences ever renders in a bundle); "pick the other rule to compare" as a task-framing confusion (adjacency: the tag "— how it's figured now" sits on the very card); the blocked reason "wearing the clothes of the disclosures" (it echoes the CTA verbatim and names the control that unblocks it).

**Card 1 — THE BLOCKED REASON + THE LOOK, both sheets: PILOT-CLEARED.** Ships.

**Card 2 — THE TALL SHEET OPENS WITH ITS ACTIONS ROW BELOW ITS OWN SCROLL at his seat: SOFT-FLAG, lane tone, medium (spouse HIGH; copy-law, calm, hunter medium — all survived).** Verified: the dialog box is y 24–767 inside 791 (fold.json), so the PAGE never scrolls it; the body scrolls internally (`sheetShell.css` max-height 94dvh + overflow-y auto, no sticky footer) and at 1536×791 the first frame ends under "For how many years" — the new reason span, the Apply and Close all sit below the cut; the only more-below cue is the scroll shadow (measured 250,247,242 → 237,235,230). The phone is worse. Pre-existing family shape (the sheets scrolled before this change), but the change seated its own sentence off-frame on arrival. **Routed: register, new Tier 3 entry (his eye or a council — a sticky actions row, a scroll cue, or nothing).**

**Card 3 — nits, recorded on the CLOSED entry:** the typed-past face's dashed Apply has no sentence DIRECTLY above it (the alert sits ~95 px up across the reserved 4 rem preview box; AT gets it via the pointer; the hunter's kill stands — one reason, one frame) · fields commit on BLUR, so the reason stays over a field visibly holding "40,000" until blur, and on commit the span unmounts with no reserved slot (the actions row shifts one line — the goal picker shares the shape) · the blocked sentence wears `.field-help`'s register (text-sm, muted) and can read as a seventh disclosure on the health sheet (calm; the refuter killed the stronger form).

**Card 4 — APPLY GOES LIVE THE INSTANT THE FIELDS VALIDATE, before the comparison lands: HARD-FLAG-shaped, lane both, high (hunter, survived — in-bundle proof: healthnc's Roth door renders "Working out both futures…" beside a NOT-disabled "Add this to my plan").** A press there commits a conversion whose two futures the household never saw. Pre-existing (Apply was gated on the candidate alone before this change) and a FRAMING fork — see-before-you-commit, or commit-any-time with the preview informational. **PARKED-FOR-BRIGGSY; register Tier 3 (the same new entry as Card 2, second bullet).**

**Card 5 — THE FAMILY'S OTHER TWO PRIMARIES (outside focus, inside the family; spouse + hunter, survived):** the budget sheet's blocked *"Use this budget"* (door 2, an empty budget) is a grey underlined link twinned with Cancel — same shape, weight, underline; tint alone says one cannot commit, and NO reason renders at either viewport (aria: `[disabled]`) — the Card 14a class on a FOURTH sheet (the Card 9 emphasis flip of 2026-08-02 is the context, not a kill); and SequencingControl's *"Use this order"* is LIVE with the applied order checked on arrival — the same precondition the health sheet BLOCKS, answered the opposite way. **Routed: register, new Tier 2 entry (pilot).**

**Card 6 — OUTSIDE FOCUS, the Roth sheet's own copy (spouse; every refuter survived):** *"you pay the tax now"* — no dollar anywhere for the conversion's tax, while the preview says "doesn't look to change much — about 9 of 10 either way": read as a free move (lane both, high — the sheet-side twin of Card 11b's unpriced strategy) · *"The income surcharge a conversion can trip two years later is now part of these numbers"* names no trip-point income, no cost, and "is now part" reads as a builder's note (high, tone) · *"a lower-tax window before required withdrawals begin"* dates neither end though the tool holds both birth years, then asks the reader to author exactly that window (medium) · the NIIT unglossed (known — the Card 14 entry). **Routed: the copy-forks entry (his words).**

**Card 7 — THE ASSUMPTIONS ROW CONTRADICTS THE SHEET: HARD-FLAG, lane both, high (hunter; survived with its premise trimmed — the NIIT leg was fabricated, the state-tax leg is disclosed in the sheet).** Door 5's conversion row ends *"errs against converting, so the lever's benefit reads understated, never oversold"*, while the Roth sheet one door over discloses a modeling choice that makes a conversion "look a shade easier here than in real life" (the surcharge's drug-plan piece held flat past 2035). One optimistic omission is enough to make "never oversold" false. **Routed: register, new Tier 1 entry (pilot: the sentence; the words his).**

**Card 8 — healthnc's MEDICARE-ERA PREMIUM SENTENCE: BLOCKER, lane both — the walk's calm-but-wrong (hunter; two refuters survived it, one killed only the UNIT reading).** *"In your plan's Medicare years, premiums could run about ~$2,700 a year for your household"* — `medicareAnchor` takes the FIRST year anyone is enrolled; on this 65/63 household that year has ONE enrollee ($202.90 × 12, trended), so the figure is right for that year and WRONG for the era it is worded as: two years on both are enrolled (~$5,700 a year) plus the extras. The step card directly beneath says *"for each of you once on Medicare"* — two same-named Medicare quantities in different enrollment frames on one screen, nothing adjacent naming who is enrolled when (`healthSheetChrome.ts` `medicareAnchor` / `bothEnrolled`). Rosier than true on the number a couple repeats. **Routed: register, new Tier 0 entry (pilot; red-first on `healthnc`).**

**colorBlindCheck** — flags only: the blocked/live pair survives every arm (above); the dash is MULTIPLEXED across the blocked Apply, the "with the conversion" series and the recommended arm — not co-visible in these frames; the applied-start face (chart + blocked Apply together) is unwalked. **comprehension** — the spouse's stumbles are Cards 2, 5, 6; the first-look reader (cold) named the amount field's emptiness with no hint of a plausible figure, and the sheet covering the "both futures" it promises to update.

**prediction** — he clears the sentence and the look on both sheets without comment; he flags Card 2 at his seat in his own words ("where's the button?") before he reads any sentence; if he opens `healthnc`'s health door he calls Card 8 a lie. **disposition** — **PILOT-CLEARED** on the focus (Card 1 ships); Cards 4 / 5 / 7 / 8 routed to the register; Card 2 filed for his eye. **evidence** — `temp/caddie/2026-09-13-sheets/{seed-retired,seed-healthnc}/{real,phone}/door-*/` + `…/look/*.png`; rules pinned: 8, 18, 20, 23, 36, 37, 40.

## 2026-09-11 — THE FOUR CHART FACES + every eye item the ranked walk carried — `seed:retired` · `seed:date` · `vault:datestale` · `solve:surplus` · `solve:failing`, REAL+PHONE (the second Sonnet-5 DUAL-PANEL A/B walk: 182 agents, 0 crashes, 46 m; bundle `temp/caddie/2026-09-11-walk`, walk 10/10 + re-walk 6/6 green)

**Why it existed.** The chart text layer was rebuilt 2026-09-05 (every word is HTML over an svg that draws only geometry; three borrowed registers; measured collisions) and nothing but the gates had read the four faces since. The walk also carried every eye item the queue had parked on it: the legend swatch redraw's shape and the outer edge's strength (council 2026-09-08, with its two pre-registered questions — "point to the middle half" and a faded-column read), the aged date band's three-row block, the already-short household's goal picker with its lead OMITTED, the phone's caveat now above the doors, and the recommendation chart's above-bar-labels fork. The corpus was folded first (E15 + E16 + rule 41 from his 2026-09-06 reads — `7aaa84b9`), the harness gained plain device-scale chart crops and a truthful doors box for the fold file (same commit), and the panel ran on the improved bundle. **Every finding below was re-verified by the chair against the bundle** (copy.txt / aria.yaml / the device-scale crops) and, where it names a mechanism, against source; panel findings the chair did not verify are held back, not carded.

**The pre-registered questions, answered (Card 1).** Nine seats across five targets answered the legend questions literally. **Q4a — point to the middle half: 10 of 10 could**, every one by the same route — the darker fill nested inside the lighter one — and **not one used the swatch outlines**; six said the outlines went unnoticed, two said they MISLED ("the only dashed ink on the plot is the gridlines, so 'dashed' reads as gridline"). Pixel sweeps by four independent seats found no stroke on either plotted boundary (x=900 steps 255→122,183,232→76,135,182 with one anti-alias pixel; a 290-column sweep of both edges found zero stroke pixels). **Q4b — a faded column: none exists on any fresh seed.** The fan's fills are byte-flat to the horizon on `retired`, `date`, `surplus` (sampled x=300–1135); the cohort-fade mask never reaches its onset on these households. The only pale column anywhere is the LEFT elapsed wedge on `vault:datestale` (Plan built → Today at ~50 % opacity, the staleness encoding — bracketed by two labelled ticks and the 2024 line under the chart, chair-verified). **So the reopen trigger the council pre-registered has no referent yet: a walk on a household whose cohort actually thins is OWED before the trigger can fire either way.** **Q5 — is the outer band's edge the page's faintest boundary: NO, by measurement.** Grayscale at x=900/1000/1037: card 255 → outer 177 (2.14:1) → inner 130 (1.79:1). The INNER seam — the one that delimits "the middle half" — is the faintest meaning-bearing boundary, and both sit under the 3:1 non-text floor; the outer edge is faint but holds (long, hard, continuous — "disappears only where the sleeve is a few px tall"). The standing "outer edge faintest" flag is REFUTED and replaced by the measured pair. The colour lane FLAGS only (rule 40: his eyes passed the encoding); the numbers are recorded for the redraw.

**Card 1 — THE BAND LEGEND'S PHANTOM KEY: the walk's answer to the council's fork. READS-CLEAN on the question it was asked (the middle half is locatable from the legend alone), SOFT-FLAG on the outlines (they key nothing and misled two readers). Lane tone. Disposition: PARKED-FOR-BRIGGSY — the redraw's SHAPE is his call by the register's own words, and the walk hands him the evidence for it.** The pilot's lean, stated: **delete the two phantom outlines.** The fill does the whole job for every reader; a nested chip would add a second cue that mirrors the composition but nothing in the read asked for it, and the outlines' only measured effect was to send two readers hunting a boundary that does not exist. ⚑ NEGATIVE stands (register): never add strokes to the drawn areas to meet the legend.

**Card 2 — THE RESERVED READOUT ROW AT REST (Q9): SOFT-FLAG, lane tone, and the strongest convergence of the walk — every seat that answered Q9 on every target called the phone's row a HOLE.** Verified: inside the band card, between the age axis and the legend, ~120–140 CSS px of blank on the phone (390 CSS: axis sub-labels end ~545, legend row 1 starts ~671 — above the fold, on the first screen) against ~24–37 px on the laptop; on `vault:datestale`'s phone verdict the blank runs ~150 px under the three-row stack. It is the scrub readout's FLOW seat, reserved at its tallest so the readout never shifts the tap targets (rule 22 honoured, and the panel said so) — but at rest it carries no ink and nothing on the surface says the chart is touchable, so it reads as "a failed render", "a hole in the middle of the one chart the couple is asked to read", "orphaned space" (opus spouse/calm/cvd, sonnet cvd/calm, the hunter — across all five targets). **This is the 2026-09-06 fork (1) the pilot leaned ACCEPT on; ten fresh readers overturn that lean.** The levers, in the order the pilot recommends: **(ii) the composition fix the pilot pre-registered on 2026-09-06 — collapse a degenerate today-range ("$1.055M – $1.055M") to one figure in `composeReadoutLines`, so column 0 no longer forces the widest box past the cap and the 390 phone at 16 px earns the PLOT seat again** (picture 08's "great") — pilot-buildable under the seat law, oracle-backed by `e2e/chart-text.spec.ts`; **(iii) give the row its words at rest** — the range sentence the O3 council (2026-07-10) ratified as the AT channel's parity ("between $101k and $1.7M … most likely about $649k") is exactly what that seat exists to hold, and six seats independently asked for those dollars on the sighted surface (chair note: O3 ruled the SR sentence at parity with the SCRUB, not that the sighted surface must stay wordless at rest — this is a framing fork, not a re-litigation); **(iv) a quiet touch hint** (E12: touch users scrub the inline band directly; nothing at rest says so). (ii) is the pilot's to build; (iii)/(iv) are his.

**Card 3 — THE OFF-TRACK NUDGE QUOTES NEITHER ENDPOINT: HARD-FLAG, lane `both`, severity BLOCKER — the walk's top calm-but-wrong.** Verified verbatim on `seed:retired` `landing-worsened`, both viewports, hero line 3, above the fold: *"About $7,500 a month less would move it toward steadier ground."* The household's spending is $10,000 a month with "Each month" checked — visible ONLY inside the assumptions dialog (`panel-worsened/aria.yaml`: textbox "Household spending, all in": 10,000), ~1,800 px down a sheet behind a door. So the ask is living on ~$2,500 a month — a 75 % cut — worded as a trim, with no "from … to …", and "toward steadier ground" never claims arrival (by design: `copy.ts:2211-2216` (then — the `verdictTrimClause` docblock, replaced when `b2b77b69` reworded the clause) chose "toward" over the old "onto"). Four seats (opus spouse/copyFinder/calm + the hunter; copyFinder and the hunter at BLOCKER) filed it independently and every refuter survived it ("the sentence names no noun — $7,500 less of what?"; "a life-ending trim reads as a tune-up"). Sonnet's spouse and calm seats read the same frame and called it "reads exactly right" / "concrete" — see the diff below. **Routed: register Tier 0 (new entry) + his words.** The pilot's proposed sentence, for his ruling: *"Spending about $2,500 a month instead of $10,000 — about $7,500 less — would move it toward steadier ground."* Both endpoints in-sentence (rule 2), the direction word kept.

**Card 4 — A PRE-65 HOUSEHOLD'S VERDICT CARRIES NO STATE-TAX DISCLOSURE, ON EITHER ROUTE: HARD-FLAG, lane `both`, severity HIGH.** Chair-verified by grep of the verbatim copy: `seed-date/real/landing` and `vault-datestale/real/verdict` render ZERO occurrences of "State income tax isn't priced yet" (or any state clause); `seed-retired/real/landing` renders it. The panel read that as a ROUTE gap; the chair traced the source and it is a COUPLING: the verdict's only state clause lives inside `verdictMedicareResidual`, and both routes render that residual only when `medicarePricedNote` holds — a household that priced Medicare AND reaches no Healthcare door, i.e. all-65+ (`Result.tsx` `showMedicarePricedNote`; `ConfidenceStatement.tsx` and `FuckOffDate.tsx` both guard on it). Every household with a pre-65 member — the working date households (58/60) AND the spine's `health` seed — meets the state clause only behind the Health door and the Roth sheet ("Not counted here: state income tax…"); its verdict — "about 9 of 10 odds", "Figured under today's rules" — says nothing, priced or not. The register's Tier-0 *Unpriced states* entry never named the cohort. **Routed to the oracle: decouple the state clause from the Medicare residual — a standalone state note on every verdict, both routes; the UNPRICED words exist verbatim (the monolith's two sentences); the PRICED pre-65 arm is a copy fork (the composer's law forbids affirm-alone, and the residual's narrowing Tail is about Medicare extras — a pre-65 priced household needs its own narrowed sentence, his words); pinned by a route × cohort × pricing matrix test ({spine, date} × {all-65+, pre-65} × {unpriced, priced} → a state clause on all eight verdicts).** Pilot wiring; one sentence his.

**Card 5 — THE ACCESSIBILITY SENTENCE ON AN ALREADY-SHORT HOUSEHOLD IS ROSIER THAN THE DRAWING: HARD-FLAG, lane `correctness`, severity HIGH.** `solve-failing/real/landing/sr-only.txt` — the band figure's only spoken description — reads *"Looking about 17 years out, the savings have most likely run out."* The drawn median falls $60k → $0 inside the first year (the zero crossing at ~6 % of the plot span, ages 72→93) and the rendered hero says "runs short from the start". SOFT-FLAG, lane `both`.** Most seats: "reads as a staircase, each label on its mark, not crowded" (sonnet spouse/calm, opus copyFinder/calm). **falsePassHunt — attempted, and it did NOT come up empty.** Its catches that survived: Card 3 (the nudge), Card 4's route gap (via the datestale spouse), Card 5 (the AT sentence), Card 11 (b)(c)(d), Card 14's brokerage option, and long-term care absent from a residual list that reads closed (already filed). **colorBlindCheck — flags only.** Measured: the inner seam 1.79:1 and the outer edge 2.14:1 in grayscale (1.81 / 2.25 deutan, 1.77 / 2.00 protan), both under the 3:1 non-text floor — the two named regions part by lightness alone, and the legend's outlines are the only shape cue and they key nothing; the ladder's "Your date" ring collapses toward the grey dots under CVD (~1.1–1.3:1 by hue) and holds on the ring outline alone; the disabled "See the strategy" is separated from the live primary by tint alone; the rec chart is the high-water mark (hue + hatch + circle/triangle, the hatch surviving phone CSS scale); the verdicts carry shape + word, never hue. Rule 40: his eyes passed the encoding — nothing here re-opens it; the numbers ride the redraw.

**comprehension (the spouse walker).** `retired`: "we're okay for now, close to the line; I'd keep the answer, then peek at the Roth door" — stumbles: "the line", the two tenths (9 of 10 vs eight in ten), the reserved row. `date`: "relieved by the date, then stuck on what 'on track' means". `datestale`: "reassured, then slightly conned" by the 2024 line arriving after the confident headline; stuck on two odds for "now" (the essentials line vs the ladder's today — killed by the chair: the ladder self-labels the headline's 9 of 10 at year 7). `surplus`: "we're fine — nothing says what converting $148,000 a year costs us in tax". `failing`: "not okay; savings hit $0 by our mid-70s; I would open the assumptions, not the goal picker — I don't trust 'see recommended strategy' until it says how a strategy helps a plan that's already broke."

**routedToOracle[]** — Card 4 (date-route disclosure parity, a test) · Card 5 (the AT ruin sentence, `copy.ts:2884` + the anchor) · Card 6 (three chart-text arms: tail-vs-box, named-over-unnamed row priority, the age row) · Card 10 (nice-step ticks in `buildYTicks` + the gate fixture) · Card 14's disabled-primary pattern · Card 3's numbers (the endpoints are pilot plumbing; the words his).

**prediction (the tape's scoring hook, in his vocabulary):** he flags the phone's empty band row on sight ("what's that big gap under the chart?"); he flags "$7,500 a month less" as soon as he opens the assumptions and sees 10,000 ("less than WHAT — we spend ten grand?"); he clears the aged phone stack as "reads fine" and does not notice the tail through "Plan built" until it is pointed at, then calls it crowded; he says "delete the outlines" on the legend within seconds; he rules the failing picker "don't even show it"; and he does NOT flag the two dollar ladders unless the two charts are side by side, at which point he says "consistency" (E11).

**disposition — Cards 3, 4, 5: HARD-FLAG (pilot fixes routed; Card 3's words his). Cards 6, 7, 8, 10, 11(b–d), 12, 14: SOFT-FLAG, PILOT-CLEARED with fixes filed (the readable surfaces ship; the register carries each prescription). Cards 1, 2, 9, 11's labels fork, 13: PARKED-FOR-BRIGGSY — one framing fork each, his lean stated.** Q8 (the phone's caveat above the doors) READS-CLEAN on every target and both panels — the July inversion is closed on the rendered surface. Q1/Q2 (the chart words as one piece; the hidden interim tick) READS-CLEAN with the Card 6 defects as the only seams found.

**evidence** — bundle `temp/caddie/2026-09-11-walk/{seed-retired,seed-date,vault-datestale,solve-surplus,solve-failing}/{real,phone}/` (regenerable; the harness increments are `7aaa84b9`); panel run `wf_2310fdb5-43b` (182 agents, 0 crashes, 46 m); the pilot's own crops read before the panel returned: `seed-retired/real/landing/{viewport,crop-band}.png`, `seed-retired/phone/landing/fullpage.png`, `seed-date/real/landing/{viewport,fullpage,crop-ladder}.png`, `vault-datestale/{real,phone}/verdict/crop-band.png`, `solve-surplus/real/committed/{viewport,cvd-grayscale-recviz-1}.png`, `solve-failing/real/goalpicker/crop-panel.png`. runs out on the 320 arm. He read the eight captures (`INDEX.md` names them): **01 / 02 / 03 crowded** (the

most of the plot); **08 great** (the 390 phone at root-16, in-plot); **04 / 05 ok** (the ladder's "on track"
column at 320, touching the rung-7 anchor at root-20); **06 / 07 crowded** (the ceiling crown beside its dot,
printing across the year dots at 320 AND at 1536). The pilot's lean on the readout (leave the plot for a
reserved flow row) and on the crown (lift it) matched; the dissent's `min-width: min-content` is dead.
**Built the same day** (architecture §12 carries the law; the oracles ride `e2e/chart-text.spec.ts` with nine
plants): the readout's MEASURED seat per width, the crown above-or-flow with the BESIDE branch deleted, the
ladder column ACCEPTED as rendered and pinned (6 px overprint / 16 px gutter at 320 × root-20).
**Two forks the build surfaced, for his second read** (`temp/cold-read-320-after/` re-shoots the same eight):

oracle because column 0's degenerate today-range needs a box 31 px wider than the cap, and the seat is per

plot's top, where a scrub rule at its own column passes behind it — pre-existing, bounded by the marks
oracle (pilot's lean: leave it, watch it).

## 2026-09-06 — HIS EYE on the AS-BUILT rewrite's one user-facing copy change: the annuity "tax-free part" help (`src/ui/copy.ts:424`, `incomeTaxableHelp`) — CALM

Not a Caddie card — a pilot-flagged read. The rewrite (`85fb5dc1`) corrected the direction word in the
annuity help: the old text called holding the entered tax-free share steady for life *"a conservative
simplification"*; the R40 decision record rules that direction OPTIMISTIC (once the paid-in amount would
have been recovered, the real stream turns fully taxable). The shipped sentence now ends *"— the tool holds
that share steady for life, which reads a little better than reality once the paid-in amount would have been
recovered."* Flagged for his eye in the b9-6 hand-off and read 2026-09-06 with no annuity knowledge (his own
framing — exactly the reader the calm law is written for). **His read: "feels calm."** Nothing owed. Tape:
the pilot predicted calm; the eye agreed.

## 2026-08-01 (late) — THE FORMAL PANEL CHAIRING the earlier walk left owed: DOORS 1 + 4, the CVD arms, the `sr-only` channel and the phone rhythm — `vault:rec` · `vault:recold`, both viewports (re-walk 4/4 green; bundle `temp/caddie/2026-08-01-21-22-00`, **⚠️ squeaky clears `temp/` — the verbatims below are the durable record**)

**Why it existed.** The morning's Card 8 was an honest PILOT read of the captures, and said so: *"a source read is not a cold read… doors 1 and 4, the CVD arms, the `sr-only` channel and the phone rhythm are UNREAD by any oracle."* This is that panel — seven fresh-context lenses (first-look · naive-spouse · copy-law→refuter · calm/honesty · CVD · phone-rhythm · false-PASS hunter), de-authored inputs, corpus-calibrated except the cold first-look.

**Card 9 — `door-1`, THE BUDGET SHEET'S EMPTY STATE: HARD-FLAG, lane `both`, severity BLOCKER.** **Seven of seven lenses flagged this face independently** — the strongest convergence any panel has produced here — and the chair's source read makes it worse than any of them stated.

- **WHAT RENDERS.** `aria.yaml:47` is `- list` **with no children**; `:49` is `button "Use this budget"` **with no `[disabled]`**. The dialog reads *"Mark which is which, and the answer takes each on its own terms."* above an empty box holding one sentence — *"Your answer uses about $78,000 a year."*
- **⚑ THE CHAIR'S FINDING, VERIFIED IN SOURCE AND NOT VISIBLE TO ANY READER: the loud primary is a NO-OP that does exactly what Cancel does.** `BudgetBuilder.tsx:267-282` — `apply()` with `items.length === 0` returned (before `eb63a9bd`) `onEscape()` when a budget governs, else `onClose()`; `budgetGoverns` is `budget !== undefined` (`budgetModel.ts:50-54`) and this household has none. So a full-width dark primary and a quiet text link are **functionally identical**, and nothing on the surface says so. That is insight 100's exact violation: the gesture promised an affordance, so it owes a rendered outcome. It renders none.
- **⚠️ THIS IS NOT A PLANT ARTIFACT — it is the UNIVERSAL FIRST-OPEN STATE.** The budget door is the invite to *build* a budget, so every household that has not built one meets exactly this frame. The reach is every user, not the `rec` seed.
- **THE EMPHASIS IS INVERTED AGAINST THE WORK.** The one path forward, `Add a line`, is the lightest ink on the sheet (phone: 63×14px of underlined text) while the no-op CTA is 278×52px filled. The naive-spouse and first-look lenses both reported the same first read: *"this didn't load."*
- **⚑ THE EMPTY BOX IS CORRECT ENGINEERING — DO NOT "FIX" IT BY REMOVING THE RESERVATION.** `BudgetBuilder.tsx:345-347` reserves that box deliberately (insight 035: it is a `role=status` that updates on every commit and sits *above* the tap targets, so it must never change height). The defect is that the reserved box is unexplained and the CTA is undifferentiated — not that the space exists. Trading a real CLS/tap-target guarantee for tidiness would be a bad swap.

**Card 10 — `door-4`, THE ASSUMPTIONS PANEL: SOFT-FLAG, lane `both`.** The panel's *tone* is the best on the product and several lenses said so unprompted — it discloses the DIRECTION of its own error (*"That choice errs against converting, so the lever's benefit reads understated, never oversold"*), which is the opposite of calm-but-wrong. Three residuals:

- **(a) THE UNIT TOGGLE CONTRADICTS ITSELF IN ONE SENTENCE — lane `both`, medium.** `copy.ts:1119` (then) (replaced 2026-08-03 by `assumptionPeriodHelp`, `copy.ts:1603-1604`; post-mortem `copy.ts:1577-1600`): *"Only how that figure is entered and shown — the plan is figured in yearly terms underneath, and switching this never changes the amount."* The field reads `6,500` with `Each month` selected. The first clause says the display changes; the last says nothing changes. A reader who flips to `Each year`, sees `78,000`, and trusts the literal clause concludes the tool moved their spending by 12×. Two lenses hit this independently. **Say what is PRESERVED, not what is unchanged.**
- **(b) THE PART A PREMIUM — lane `correctness`, medium, and the ambiguity runs in the DANGEROUS direction.** The verdict page asserts *"The rare Part A premium stays inside the spending you gave us."* The spending help says *"Leave Medicare premiums out **entirely**: Part B, its income surcharge, and any Part D, Medigap, or Medicare Advantage premium."* The colon defines the set and Part A is not in it — but **"entirely" signals exhaustiveness**, so a literal reader omits a premium the verdict page believes is included. Rare (most households get Part A premium-free on 40 quarters) but ~$500+/month for one that does not. **Routed to the oracle.**
- **(c) NO VISIBLE EXIT AT FIRST PAINT.** `fold.json` puts the dialog at 24→767 of a 791px frame; `Close` is the LAST node after ~20 rows. Door 1 shows `Cancel` beside its primary; door 4 shows nothing — and its own header says *"Edits land as you leave each field,"* so a reader who nudges a value and wants out has already committed it.

**Card 11 — THE CVD SCREEN ON `door-4` IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND: routed to the ORACLE, not a tone finding.** The face carries **24 controls** (`radio`/`textbox` in `aria.yaml`); the dialog scrolls INTERNALLY while `cvd-*.png` captures only the above-fold frame — so roughly the first four are screened and **~20 are captured in no arm at all**, normal or simulated. The unscreened set includes the state picker and the Medicare-extras radios, i.e. precisely the *selected-control* faces the never-color-alone law exists for. **The harness already knows this shape** — increment 5 added `cvd-<arm>-state-picker.png` for exactly this reason, but only on the `intake:fork` walk; the vault door walk inherited none of it. *(This is the same family as S6's "the face was never walked because the walk crashed on it": a screen that reports clean because it cannot see.)* **What WAS screened passes** — the band holds three distinct luminance steps in grayscale (outer / inner / near-black median) and the `Each month | Each year` segment marks selection with weight + fill + ring, not hue.

**Card 12 — PHONE RHYTHM: SOFT-FLAG, lane `tone`.** Door 1's readout is a 347×120px card holding one 20px line — **~83px of blank white at the visual centre of the sheet** (y 584–666), which is where the "didn't load" read comes from. Door 4's phone fold lands on a *completed sentence* with only an 8px shadow as the more-below tell, where the laptop gets a half-clipped section heading; and the survivor label orphans its final word (`today's` alone on line 2, directly above the `75 %` input, reading as a truncation).

**falsePassHunt — attempted, and it did NOT come up empty.** Its blocker is Card 9. Its other live catch is the already-filed record-card verb, now with **new evidence the morning's card did not have: the contradiction co-renders in a SINGLE FRAME** — `door-4`'s *"Withdrawal order — A little from each"* is visible in the same viewport as the record card's *"It still matches your plan as it stands today."*, not one flick apart. Routes to the record card's half (2), **Briggsy's ruling, unchanged.**

**⚑ CHAIR-KILLED against the bundle — four of the copy-law lens's findings died on refutation, and two more the chair killed itself.** The refuters killed: the "Saved to this device" / "No backup file" contradiction; the state-tax double-count; *"$78,000 understates the true annual draw"*; and the fan's *"23 years out"* vs the `93/92` axis. **The chair additionally killed the false-pass hunter's sharpest-sounding claim:** that *"It sits close to the line"* rendering IDENTICALLY under `rec`'s **"On track — 9 of 10"** and `recold`'s **"On the line — 7 of 10"** is a mis-fire. It is not — `copy.ts:3598` documents `verdictHoldClause` as covering *"direction 'on-the-line' — borderline (**or on-track with a rough downside**)"*, so both firings are by design. **What survives from it is narrower and real:** *"the line"* is a threshold the surface never names, on the hero and again in the details door — so a 9-of-10 reader gets a sentence they cannot distinguish from a 7-of-10 reader's. Lane `tone`, and it is the one finding three separate lenses reached by different routes.

**colorBlindCheck** — see Card 11: what is screened passes; the screen's COVERAGE is the defect, and it is filed against the harness rather than the product.

**prediction (the tape's scoring hook, in his vocabulary):** he flags door 1 within about four seconds of opening it — *"why is there a big empty box and what does the green button even do?"* — and he is right to. He likely does NOT flag door 4's tone (it is the most honest copy on the product) but does trip on the month/year sentence if he touches the toggle. He is unlikely to notice the CVD coverage gap by eye, which is exactly why it is filed as an oracle defect rather than a tone one.

**disposition — Card 9: HARD-FLAG (blocks until fixed; it is pre-existing and universal, not shipped by this session). Cards 10, 12: PILOT-CLEARED with fixes filed. Card 11: routed to the oracle.** No framing forks and no one-way doors, so nothing here parks — except the record-card verb, which was already Briggsy's.

**✓ CLOSED 2026-08-02 — all three actionable cards shipped.** **Card 9** in two halves: the MECHANISM (`eb63a9bd` — the CTA no longer silently does what Cancel does; `aria-disabled` + an announced reason, Chromium-verified at opacity 0.55, still operable) and then the SHAPE on **Briggsy's live ruling "flip it"** (`cddcdb25` — "Add a line" takes the primary on the empty frame, "Use this budget" drops to quiet and they trade back the instant a line exists). **Card 10(b)** (`c27e7951`) — fixed at the ASK, not the verdict: softening the verdict alone would have left the premium landing nowhere. **Card 11** (`ed348492`) — the crops land, and running the walk to verify them exposed that `walkDoors` had been RED on its own default target for an unknown number of sessions. ⚠️ **Two corrections to this card's own text, from opening source:** the `BudgetBuilder.tsx:267-282` anchor below EXCLUDES the `onClose()` at `:259` that was the entire defect (deleted by `eb63a9bd`; `apply()` carries no `onClose()` today), and the reserved-box fence points at the wrong file — the reservation is `min-block-size: 7.5rem` in `budget.css`, and it is a FLOOR (≈120px empty → ≈198px full), not the fixed height insight 035 describes. **Since closed:** door 4's month/year sentence (`copy.ts:1475` then; FIXED 2026-08-03 as `assumptionPeriodHelp`). **The prediction above is PARTLY SCORED on the tape (2026-09-06):** door 1 a HIT on his 2026-08-02 "flip it" ruling; door 4 (its tone and the month/year sentence) stays awaiting his read, and the month/year sentence was replaced 2026-08-03, so re-walk before scoring it.

**evidence** — bundle `temp/caddie/2026-08-01-21-22-00/{vault-rec,vault-recold}/{real,phone}/`; source verified by the chair at `BudgetBuilder.tsx:267-282` + `:345-347`, `budgetModel.ts:50-54`, `copy.ts:1237` (then — since replaced, see the ✓ CLOSED note above) + `:3598`.

## 2026-08-01 — THE `rec` / `recold` DOOR WALK, the two plants whose doors no oracle had ever opened — `vault:rec` · `vault:recold`, both viewports (walk 4/4 green; bundle `temp/caddie/2026-08-01-02-52-21`, **since cleared at squeaky — the verbatims below are the durable record, transcribed BEFORE deletion**)

**Why it existed:** the door-walk allowlist gained `'rec'`/`'recold'` in `7e131330`, but the walk itself had never run, so S6's Cards 6–7 could judge the record card's landing verb and **nothing behind its doors**. The queue had flagged this as a live coverage gap and explicitly cautioned against guessing the door set.

**Two measured answers to questions the queue left open** — both had been filed as unverifiable by inspection, and both are now measured rather than assumed: `recold`'s door set is **IDENTICAL** to `rec`'s (parity, not divergence — the caution that the superseded card might reseat the quiet row did not bear out), and it is **FOUR** doors, not the unverified "five": split · withdrawal-order · roth · assumptions. **No health door on this base.**

**Card 8 — `vault:rec`, THE HOLDS CARD READS AS AN EXECUTION CLAIM THE DOOR BENEATH IT REFUTES: HARD-FLAG, lane `both`, severity BLOCKER (calm-but-wrong).** Verified verbatim, same frame: the record card states *"It still matches your plan as it stands today."* while **door 2, one flick below, labels the household's current order *"A little from each — your current order"*** (proportional). The saved record's winner is **`taxable-first` with `noChange: false`** (`devSeeds.ts:1885-1899`) — so the remembered advice was **SWITCH to Brokerage first, and the household never did it.**

*"Still matches" is engine-true* — it means the record remains RE-PRESENTABLE (fingerprint + solver version + era all unmoved). **But a household reads "matches your plan" as "I am doing this", and the next door shows they are not.** The error direction is the rosy one: a couple who never executed the conversion reads confirmation that they did.

**This is the exact MIRROR of the verb fixed in `532cad82`.** There, a claim about SHOWING (*"We can no longer show this as current"*) read as *the advice still stands*. Here, a claim about RE-PRESENTABILITY reads as *already executed*. Same family, opposite arm — and **`recold` is CLEAN on this axis**, which is why one fix could not have caught both.

**disposition: HARD-FLAG — does not ship.** It sharpens the case for the record card's **half (2)** (naming the saved strategy in frame), which is a **BRIGGSY RULING, not a pilot fix**, because it amends his own written ruling 3 (`act4-u17-s5-execution-plan.md:38-48`). Filed in `TODO.md` under the record-card prescription. **⚠️ Note for whoever builds it: the chaired "names no conversion" complaint is NOT buildable and NOT a gap** — `SavedRecommendationActionV3` carries no conversion field by design (`model.ts:2324-2329`) and the live ranking is sequencing-only. *(⚑ 2026-10-16 doc audit: the last clause was already false on this entry's date — conversion candidates rank live since 2026-07-19 (the trend sourcing unit, council `wf_c673339e-257`; `solve.ts`'s header at `0340f5cc`), and the docblock cited here records the no-conversion-field rationale as EXPIRED that day, so a crowned arm CAN carry a real conversion.)*

**evidence:** commit `292cf9bd` (CI 30681007801 green) carries the walk's own report; bundle cleared at the 2026-08-01 squeaky, **deliberately transcribed here first** — the 2026-07-27 batch lost its rendered verbatims to exactly that deletion and the lesson is now paid twice.

## 2026-07-27 — U17 §S6: THE ARRIVED SURFACE'S FIRST COLD READ + FOUR FACES NO ORACLE HAD EVER SEEN — `vault:datearrived` (new plant) · `vault:datestale` · `vault:rec` · `vault:recold` · `seed:datesplit` · `seed:datemixed`, both viewports (panel wf_efc3968f-cf5 — **140 agents, ZERO crashes**; bundle `temp/caddie/2026-07-27-22-37-39`)

**⚠️ ALL SIX CARDS ARE HARD-FLAG. NONE SHIPS.** The batched-oracle grant clears *readable* surfaces; it has never covered **calm-but-wrong**, and this batch found that class on three faces. The findings below are **pre-existing product defects**, not S6 regressions — S6's contribution was building the plant that finally made them visible. The stage's value turned out to be the *looking*, not the artifact.

**Chair discipline note:** every finding carded below was **re-verified by the chair against the bundle (and, where it names a mechanism, against source)**. Panel findings the chair did NOT independently verify are held as panel-raw and are NOT carded — they are listed as such at the end. A raw panel output is never a clearance, and a chaired card must not launder unverified volume into apparent consensus.

**Card 1 — `seed:datesplit`, TWO ODDS FOR ONE MOMENT: HARD-FLAG, lane `both`, severity BLOCKER (4 independent lens formulations).** Verified verbatim, same frame: `landing/copy.txt:9` — *"The essentials alone are covered about 2 years out — around 2028 — **about 9 of 10 odds**."* against `landing/aria.yaml` — *"Stopping in 2 years: **about 4 of 10 — below the line**."* Same year, same household, two odds, and **`grep -c` for "full lifestyle" / "whole budget" / "all-in" in that copy returns 0** — nothing on the surface tells the reader the ladder prices the FULL budget while the prose prices essentials. The rosier figure is the 44px prose; the contradicting one is a hover/aria channel. A reader reconciling them either takes the friendly number or concludes the tool is broken. **This is the cardinal sin by name.** routedToOracle: yes — correctness lane, filed.

**Card 2 — THE MONEY CHART IS THE ESSENTIALS-ONLY WORLD UNDER A FULL-LIFESTYLE HEADLINE: HARD-FLAG, lane `both`, BLOCKER — converged across THREE faces (`datesplit`, `datemixed`, `datearrived`).** Verified on `datearrived`: headline `verdict/copy.txt:2` names *"the fuck-off date around 2025"*; the only chart's caption `:33` reads *"This range follows the essentials-covered date."* — which names a **DATE and never a SPENDING LEVEL**. On `datesplit` the gap is $74k all-in vs ~$46k essentials, visible only inside door 1. So the reader binds a rising median and an $8M plume to *their* life, when the picture is a materially cheaper one. **⚑ THIS IS BRIGGSY'S FORK, ALREADY RAISED WITH HIM 2026-07-27 and explicitly deferred to debate:** reword the caption to name the spending level (cheap, true, leaves the hero visual still mismatched to the hero claim) **or** plot the full-lifestyle world (visual and claim finally agree; costs the essentials picture the floor line leans on). **A framing-level fork — PARKED-FOR-BRIGGSY by the grant's own exception, never a pilot copy fix.**

**Card 3 — `vault:datearrived`, THE ROTH DOOR CALLS THE HOUSEHOLD'S OWN EXECUTED CONVERSION A TYPO: HARD-FLAG, lane `both`, BLOCKER (3 independent formulations).** Verified verbatim in `door-3-revisit-your-roth-conversion/aria.yaml`: the applied plan pre-fills (`34,000` / `2024` / `4` years), a `role=alert` fires **on open** — *"That year has already passed — this plan can start the conversions in 2026 or later."* — and **`button "Add this to my plan" [disabled]`**. One door over, `door-5/dialog.txt` states the same plan as live fact: *"Converting ~$34,000 a year for 4 years, started in 2024."* Mechanism verified in source: `RothLever.tsx:143-149` re-seeds the applied plan on open; `:161` runs `startYearPassed` against it with no distinction between a year the reader TYPED and their own executed history. §S1's own comment (`:49-50`) had already FILED the mid-flight question and then shipped the refusal that makes it a broken door today. **Dated reachability: real households hit this 2027-01-01** via the plan-clock landmine. **Briggsy CLEARED the fix 2026-07-27; full prescription in `TODO.md`, including the trap** (suppressing the alert alone leaves `complete()` still returning null ⇒ a dead primary control with no rendered reason — insight 100).

**Card 4 — `vault:datearrived`, THE PREMISE LINE CONTRADICTS ITS OWN AXIS: HARD-FLAG, lane `both`, HIGH (3 lenses).** Verified: `verdict/copy.txt:35` — *"Where today sits on it is undetermined until you re-confirm."* against `verdict/aria.yaml` — `img "Today — ages 63 and 63": Today 63 / 63`. The sentence says undetermined; the chart draws and labels it. **The error direction is the rosy one:** lenses reported reading the sentence as a bug and trusting the tick, which makes the six-year modeled segment read as lived history. routedToOracle: yes.

**Card 5 — `seed:datemixed`, THE REFUSAL DENIES A DATE THE SAME SCREEN PLOTS: HARD-FLAG, lane `both`, BLOCKER.** Verified: the landing plots `Essentials date 64 / 65` (aria) and states *"The essentials alone are covered about 6 years out — around 2032 — about 9 of 10 odds"* (`copy.txt:6`), while all three lever doors refuse with *"anchors to your **work-optional date**, so it needs one on the board first."* A date **is** on the board. Worse, `grep -c "work-optional date" landing/copy.txt` = **0** — the product says *fuck-off date*; the refusal invents a term the reader has never seen. **⚑ CHAIR'S OWN MISS, RECORDED:** the pilot cleared this copy earlier the same session from a SOURCE read, calling it "honest and correct" and attributing the walk failure wholly to the harness. The harness was broken *and* the copy is false on this face. **A source read is not a cold read — the exact failure this session kept finding in other people's filings, committed by the chair.** The three-in-a-row rhythm question raised with Briggsy is now subordinate to this: the sentences are not merely repetitive, they are wrong here.

**Cards 6–7 — `vault:rec` / `vault:recold`, the S5 record card's first human-visible read: HARD-FLAG, lane `both`, HIGH/BLOCKER (panel-converged, chair-verified on the `recold` verb).** Verified on `recold`: the card says *"We can no longer show this as current."* — a verb about **SHOWING**, not about the advice being **WRONG**. A household still executing the saved conversions reads the display as stale and the advice as standing. Its two causes sit below the 791 fold, so the only line in frame is the one that understates. On `rec`, the converged panel finding (chair-verified as present in copy): the card asserts *"It still matches your plan as it stands today"* while **naming no strategy at all** — no order, no conversion, no amount, no saved date — so the reader is asked to accept a claim whose subject they must recall from memory, with no details home on the surface. routedToOracle: the `recold` verb is an honesty defect, filed.

**falsePassHunt:** attempted and **found**, repeatedly — this batch is the hunter lane's largest yield to date. Every blocker above is a rosier-than-true reading available to a scared couple, and three of them (Cards 1, 2, 5) are reachable without opening a single door.

**colorBlindCheck (flags only, never clean-verdict weight):** the arrived band's elapsed segment is carried by **opacity alone** — the legend names 3 encodings while the plot draws 5, and the ghosted elapsed band and median have no key. In grayscale the elapsed wedge reads as a fourth unexplained band. The one genuinely NEW signal this stage introduced ("these years already happened") is the one with no text channel. **Flagged, routed, not carded as a verdict.**

**The four parked aged-surface tone calls — the panel's answers (advisory; his eye still owns them):** (1) *elapsed-segment de-emphasis* — well calibrated as a wash (pale, hard step + dashed rule at Today, survives every CVD arm), but risks the opposite failure: faint enough that its left edge reads as a real historical balance. (2) *the premise line* — undermined by Card 4, not by its wording. (3) *the arrived idiom* — **lands**; multiple lenses called *"penciled the fuck-off date around 2025 — that year has already come and gone"* warm, hedged and blameless. (4) **the WITHDRAWN work-stops marker — the panel's answer is that the silence reads MISSING, not restrained.** Verified: the headline names 2025 in 44px type and the axis carries no 2025 mark at all (ticks run `Plan built 57/57 · 60/60 · Today 63/63 · 70/70 · 80/80 · 93/93`), so the reader must derive that 2025 sits between two age ticks. The §S2.1 hawk veto forbade a **future-tense named marker left of Today** — it did not require silence, and a PAST-TENSE mark remains available within the veto. **His call; the mechanism is fit-pinned either way.**

**prediction (the tape's hook):** he stops on Card 1 within seconds of hovering the ladder — a visible 9-vs-4 on one screen is his "is this broken?" register, and the two-time-bases precedent (2026-07-10) is the same shape one axis over. Card 2 he engages as a *design* question rather than a bug, exactly as he already did unprompted. Card 3 he reads as plainly broken ("why is it yelling at me about my own conversion?"). Cards 6–7 draw rule-38 detail-hunger — *"show me what strategy we're talking about"* — his standing lens since 2026-07-11. The arrived idiom itself passes unremarked, and the missing 2025 mark is the likeliest thing he asks for by name.

**disposition:** **HARD-FLAG ×6 — none ships.** Card 2 is additionally **PARKED-FOR-BRIGGSY** (framing fork, the grant's stated exception). Card 3 is **CLEARED-TO-FIX** by him, filed with its trap. Cards 1, 4, 5 route to the build lane as correctness defects.

**evidence:** bundle `temp/caddie/2026-07-27-22-37-39` (six targets × real+phone; `datearrived` re-walked into the same run stamp after joining the vault door allowlist in `ed6432cd` — without it the stage's own deliverable would have been chaired on its landing alone, the 2026-07-10 coverage lesson one door down) · panel journal wf_efc3968f-cf5 · source binds `RothLever.tsx:46-67,121-141` · commits `7e113b6e` `58076ba6` `c55913b0` `65b306e7` `ed6432cd` `9249e4d4`. **Corpus fold-in: no NEW rule minted — every blocker here is an existing rule firing (rule 38 detail-hunger, insight 100's promise/affordance pair, insight 101's predicate-extension law, the cardinal calm-but-wrong bar). The corpus was already current (last real Briggsy read 2026-07-11, folded 2026-07-12; every row since is unscored).**

**Panel-raw, NOT carded (chair did not independently verify; listed so nothing is silently dropped):** the `datestale` essentials/ladder odds collision (same shape as Card 1, likely the same root); both aged faces' door-preview charts drawing the elapsed years at full weight with no Today tick; the re-entry gate never printing the vault's vintage before asking for attestation; the phone-tier clipping of the `datemixed` refusal mid-sentence at the sheet edge; the aria figure name describing the arrived chart as "the years ahead". **These are real-looking and cheap to verify — verify before acting, per insight 105.** (Registered 2026-09-25 in `docs/backlog.md` under *Caddie walk-and-chair debt on four changed or never-capturable faces*.)

## 2026-07-23 (later) — THE MEDIAN-ADVANTAGE + STEER increment's first cold read, AND THE FIRST SONNET-5 DUAL-PANEL A/B — `solve:steer` (the no-pretax refusal, first ever) · `solve:surplus` (the delta qualifier's first live read) at both viewports (commits 8b9cab61 + 0b75740f; panel wf_91a3fa9a-b2e — 47 agents ZERO crashes: 5 perception seats × 2 models per target + one Opus hunter each, Opus refuters; bundles `temp/caddie/2026-07-23-19-44-31` (before) + `…20-19-22` (chair-fix re-walk); the walk's fold.json arbitrated the one confirmed layout observation)

**What this batch judges:** (1) the no-pretax steer — a household whose savings hold no rankable pre-tax dollars picks a goal and gets a calm named-reason refusal instead of a solve; (2) the hero's typical-future qualification — "The "$280,000 more" above is an average across the futures we tested. In the typical future the edge is closer to about $190,000." beside the level's typical-outcome line; (3) the ratified Sonnet-5 dual-panel A/B's first evidence.

**Card 1 — `solve:steer`, the refusal face: PILOT-CLEARED with ONE chair fix (the tightened note), live re-walked.** The focus question PASSED on both panels — the Opus spouse: *"THE REFUSAL IS DIRECTIONALLY HONEST AND CALM. ## 2026-07-10 — `?vault=retired`: the re-entry gate, fresh return (U13 batch — the gate read-back + the button pair)

**States walked:** unlock (passphrase pre-filled) → gate (read-back, NO clock notes — same-day
save, correct) → affirm → verdict. **Viewports:** REAL 1536×791@2.5dpr + PHONE 390×844@3.
**Readers:** 7 fresh contexts (first-look · naive-spouse · copy-law finder→refuter ·

matched the DOM verbatim (headline, all three figures, both button labels, the disclaimer).

almost letterpress-like… like a good advisor pausing to make sure we're working from the
truth."* Gut trust: yes. Phone: *"unmistakably the same product."*

**toneVerdict: SOFT-FLAG** — the gate's bones read right; two wording finds would reward his eye.

### Findings

1. **SOFT-FLAG [tone] — "Markets and paychecks move" is off-audience for an all-retired
   household.** The spouse walker's PRIMARY stumble (*"we don't HAVE paychecks — does this
   thing even know we stopped working?"*) + finder-stands-refuter-confirmed (rule 3; the
   em-dash prong was killed — no dollar rides the sentence). `reentryIntro` is ONE route-blind
   string (`copy.ts`). Candidate: a route-aware variant for the spine route ("Markets and
   benefit checks move…"). Tweak path `copy.ts` `reentryIntro`.
2. **SOFT-FLAG [both] — "Social Security, monthly at full retirement age" is ambiguous for a
   couple already claiming.** Refuter-confirmed (rule 8 prong): a household that claimed at 62
   or 70 receives a different check than the FRA figure, and the gate's own question ("Are
   these still your numbers?") invites comparison against the real check — they can "correct"
   a right number or okay a benchmark they can't verify. The figure IS the honest stored input
   (the intake asks at-FRA); the gate just doesn't say which frame it's in. Candidate: one
   gloss clause ("the statement figure your plan models from").
3. **Presented, not flagged — the affirm-default tension (HIS batch question: the button
   pair).** The Hunter's rosier reading: *"we just looked, it said that keeps our reading
   honest — so our answer is current and safe to act on"* — the glance + big-green-affirm can
   feel like a freshness stamp though it records nothing and re-prices nothing. Design side,
   on the record: the gate is a PROMPT never an attestation (council constraint (c)), the
   read-back exists exactly so the affirm is informed, and "Still about right" hedges.
   Both hunters (fresh + stale) hit the same shape independently. His ruling.
4. **Staged (comprehension notes):** "show my answer" — the answer to WHAT is never named on
   the gate (first-look sat with it); Alex/Sam's relationship is inferred, never labeled; the
   spouse-who-didn't-build-it presses affirm with a knot ("I'm being asked to certify numbers
   I never typed") — product-reality, named for the record, no cheap fix.

**falsePassHunt:** attempted — found the freshness-stamp reading (#3). Nothing on the gate
overstates safety in figures; the hunt's survivor is a feeling, not a number.

**colorBlindCheck:** clean as screened (never a clearance) — achromatic by construction, no
hue carries meaning; primary/secondary split is fill-vs-underline + wording; every figure is
a dt/dd text pair in the aria tree.

**comprehension:** the spouse walker derived the situation correctly and chose affirm for the
right reason (same-day save). Trust verdict: *"the bones are honest: the gate REFUSES to show
a verdict off stale numbers."*

**capture notes:** the phone gate scrolls by design (901 vs 844) — the first-look's "clipped
footer" is the fold, not a clip; demoted.

**prediction (tape hook):** he clears the gate's bones on sight — the read-back + the button
pair are his own ratified shape, and the register reads calm. The likeliest stop is
"paychecks" ("we're retired — why paychecks?"); the FRA frame he asks about as a comprehension

1. **SOFT-FLAG [both] — "fund snapshots" is the one jargon phrase among three plain notes**
